Tengo

Independent expert review

Product, technology, privacy, legal, risk, data, design, accessibility, behaviour, language, commercial and judging perspectives

Subject: Tengo, a money app for people with irregular income, entered in the FNB App of the Year hackathon (Financial inclusion and money confidence).

State reviewed: commit d114497, reviewed 2026-10-20.

Nature of the review: a structured review by thirteen simulated professional perspectives, run as independent agents, with a synthesis. No reviewer is or claims to be FNB staff. Nothing here is legal or financial advice. All app data is fictional.

Files: docs/review/ (REPORT.pdf, findings.csv, ROADMAP.md, risk-register.md, judge-qa.md, model.xlsx, backtest/, reviewer files).

1Executive summary

What this review could not establish. Real user behaviour or comprehension (no user sessions took place); forecast accuracy on real bank data (the backtest uses synthetic data written to the same assumptions as the engine); legal sign-off (the legal chapter is a simulated perspective, not legal advice); FNB's internal criteria, systems, data or pricing (all bank figures are public); real costs at scale; translation quality (no translations exist yet). Every external figure carries a source and access date or is labelled Assumption.

Verdict. Tengo is a well-built, honest prototype of a distinct idea: give people with irregular income the date their money runs out, beside the date their next money usually lands. The build is fast, accessible and carefully explained. It is not yet ready for real users or a bank pilot: the Home screen contradicts itself, the engine misses the moments that matter most for irregular earners (income that stops, payday, large bills just after payday), some outputs read as permissions or could cause harm, and nothing has been tested with real people or real data. The 13 reviewers average 5.0 of 10 (range 4 to 6); the simulated judge panel scores 39 of 70. They raised 151 findings (48 High, 66 Medium, 37 Low).

Top five strengths

  1. A distinct, date-first answer for people without a payday, with next money inferred from irregular deposits (PROD, COMM, judge Creativity 7).
  2. A transparent, deterministic engine with 97.6% unit coverage that beats a naive baseline on synthetic data (MAE 2.1 against 3.4 days).
  3. An accessible, polished interface: Lighthouse accessibility 100, one axe WCAG 2.2 violation across 15 pages, both themes.
  4. Honest labelling and a privacy-first demo: nothing leaves the device and simulated controls are marked as such.
  5. Fast on a throttled phone: Home in 1.5 s cold, 142 KB of compressed JavaScript.

Top five risks

  1. Home shows Short beside "R310 safe to spend" and a chart saying Enough (R1).
  2. Income that has stopped still counts as coming, so the app can show Enough with high confidence (R3).
  3. "What helps most" can suggest paying a car instalment late; "safe" reads as a promise (R4, R2).
  4. No users, and validation is circular on synthetic data (R8).
  5. No backend, auth or encryption: real data must not be touched yet (R12).

Top three fixes

  1. One rule drives Home ("Tight until", daily budget not advised when Short, chart labelled "if money lands as usual"): about 4 hours, the largest single score lift (JUDGE-01).
  2. Remove harmful outputs and teach the engine when a pattern breaks (no late credit or insurance, renamed safe figures, paused-income and no-income rules): about 2 days (JUDGE-03, JUDGE-04).
  3. Make every claim true (languages "English today", privacy screens as demo statements) and run a six-person comprehension test: 1 hour plus 2 days (JUDGE-02, JUDGE-07).

2Scope, method and limits

This is an independent, read-only review of Tengo as it stood at commit d114497 on 2026-10-20, following REVIEW.md. No application code, tests or configuration were changed; everything written lives in docs/review/.

2.1 What was reviewed

  • The codebase (6 747 lines of application TypeScript, 642 lines of unit tests), README, BRIEF.md and the README "Redesign" section. REVIEW.md refers to docs/BRIEF.md and docs/REDESIGN.md; those files do not exist, so the root BRIEF.md and the README sections were used.
  • The production build served locally, driven with Playwright. 264 screenshots: every page, both themes, at 390, 768 and 1280 px, for all three personas (docs/review/screens/).
  • Build, unit tests, lint, i18n, fonts and contrast checks; the full Playwright suite (221 tests); unit coverage; npm audit; bundle sizes; licence summary; Lighthouse mobile; a throttled timing of the in-app Home page.
  • A persona walkthrough and a fact sheet of what the product does today, shared with every reviewer (Section 3).

2.2 Panel method

Thirteen reviewers were run as independent agents. Twelve worked in parallel without reading each other's files, so they could not anchor on one another. The judge panel ran last and was told to use the others' evidence. Each received the same shared instructions: cite a file and line, a screenshot, a command output or a source URL for every finding; label anything unsourced as Assumption with a range; do not claim to be FNB staff; name at least three weaknesses; follow the copy rules. Two reviewers (data scientist, commercial) were interrupted by a usage limit; the data scientist's file was already complete and the commercial reviewer was resumed with its context to finish.

The synthesis checked every reviewer file for required sections, findings tables and copy rules, merged all findings into findings.csv, and verified the most consequential claims independently (Section 8.2).

2.3 Check results

CheckResult
npm run buildpass
npm run test (contrast, then 66 unit tests)pass, 116 of 116 contrast pairs
npm run lint (ESLint and dash check)pass
npm run i18n:checkpass, 11 locales with identical keys
npm run fonts:checkpass
Playwright (screens, flows, phone, axe)221 passed
Unit coverageengine 97.6% of statements; all of src 20.0%
npm audit12 advisories (2 critical, 5 high, 5 moderate); 2 moderate ship to users

2.4 Limits

Everything in the "could not establish" box in Section 1 applies. In addition: the backtest is only as good as the generator; prices and market figures move, so every one carries an access date; Lighthouse cannot reach in-app pages because it clears storage, so the in-app timing used a separate Playwright measurement with matching throttling; and LibreOffice was not available, so the financial model's 1 474 formulas were verified with the Python formulas engine (zero errors) and the workbook is set to recalculate fully when opened in Excel.

3What the product does today

Prepared from the code at commit d114497 on 2026-10-20. Every line here is checked against the source, the check outputs in this folder, or the persona snapshot. It describes what exists, not what the pitch says.

Form

  • A single page web app: React 18, TypeScript (strict), Vite, Tailwind, React Router 6, i18next, Zustand. No backend. Source: package.json, src/App.tsx.
  • All data lives in the browser's localStorage under two keys: tengo.session.v1 (language, theme, active persona, sidebar) and tengo.data.v1 (records, consent, audit log, review requests, deletion receipts). Source: src/store/session.ts:31, src/data/localRepository.ts:15.
  • Data access goes through a TengoRepository interface with one implementation, LocalRepository, which adds a 140 ms artificial delay. Source: src/data/repository.ts, src/data/index.ts.
  • Installable as a PWA with a same-origin service worker for offline use. Source: public/sw.js, src/main.tsx.
  • 6 747 lines of application TypeScript and 642 lines of unit tests. Source: wc -l over src.

Data

  • No real data. Three fictional personas (Thabo, e-hailing driver, Tshwane; Lerato, hairdresser, Mamelodi; Ayanda, spaza owner, Tembisa) with 90 days of deterministic synthetic transactions ending yesterday. Each persona's balance is chosen by the engine so it starts in Tight, Enough and Short respectively. Source: src/data/seed/generate.ts, src/data/seed/personas.ts.
  • Seed data regenerates every day unless the user imports or edits. Source: src/data/localRepository.ts getRecord.
  • A user can replace a persona's history with a CSV bank statement, parsed in the browser (PapaParse). It detects columns, skips bad rows, requires 14 days, warns under 28, and shifts statements that ended more than 3 days ago so they end yesterday. PDF import is shown as "coming next" and does not exist. Source: src/lib/csv.ts, src/pages/Import.tsx.
  • No bank connection, no open banking, no login, no accounts.

Forecast engine (src/engine/)

  • Splits history into income, recurring bills (same description, amounts within 20 percent, 24 to 36 days apart, at least 2 times) and everyday spend. Source: classify.ts:16.
  • Daily spend: median, 80th and 20th percentile of rolling 7 day spend over the last 60 days, divided by 7. Low confidence (under 28 days) widens the spread by 1.5. Source: forecast.ts:20,26.
  • Next money: deposits of at least 10 percent of typical monthly income. Weekday pattern when 60 percent land on one weekday (latest date is one day after typical), otherwise gaps between deposits (20th, 50th, 80th percentile). No pattern means a 30 day plan. Source: forecast.ts:24,28.
  • Runway: balance run down daily at each rate minus bills, ignoring future income. The date is the day the normal path reaches zero. Horizon 180 days. Source: forecast.ts:18.
  • State: Enough if the heavy path reaches the latest next money; Tight if the normal path reaches the typical next money; otherwise Short.
  • Safe to spend today: (balance minus bills before the latest next money) divided by days to it, floored at zero, rounded down to R5.
  • Also: a 30 day timeline that does include expected income, a what-if, a loan check with "safe to lend", "What helps most" (extra money, daily cut or bill to move), and a keyword-based spending breakdown. Source: forecast.ts, help.ts, spending.ts.

Persona snapshot on 2026-10-20 (from persona-snapshot.json)

Persona State Date shown Range Safe to spend Next money Usual daily spend
Thabo Tight 27 Oct 25 to 28 Oct R295 26 Oct (weekday) R295
Lerato Enough 1 Nov 26 Oct to 1 Nov R330 24 Oct (weekday) R120
Ayanda Short 23 Oct 22 to 25 Oct R310 25 Oct (interval) R623

Screens

Landing, persona picker, consent, Home (date hero, range band, state, stats, 30 day chart, What helps most, recent activity, Why this date drawer), Plan (what-if, loan check), Money (history, filters, search, corrections, recurring bills, spending breakdown), Import, Inbox (simulated WhatsApp or SMS preview in 11 languages), Privacy centre (consent toggles, data minimisation, see and export data, audit log, human review request, sample breach notice, retention, cross-border card, placeholder Information Officer, delete with receipt), Me (persona, language, theme, about, reset). Command menu on Ctrl or Cmd plus K. Source: src/App.tsx, src/pages/.

Languages

11 locale files with identical keys (577 keys). All 10 non-English files currently contain English text. A Google Cloud Translation script exists but has not been run (no API key). Source: npm run i18n:check, src/i18n/status.json, scripts/translate.mjs.

What is simulated, not real

Bank data, messaging (nothing is sent), privacy controls (labelled "Simulated privacy controls. Not a compliance certification."), the Information Officer contact (placeholder), the human review (stored locally, nobody receives it), translations, the logo and icons (placeholders), encryption claims (described for a real deployment only).

Quality signals (see checks.md, audit.md, playwright.txt, coverage.txt, lighthouse/)

  • Build, 66 unit tests, lint, i18n check, fonts check and 116 contrast pairs all pass.
  • Playwright: screenshot, flow, phone and axe accessibility suites (see playwright.txt for the count and result).
  • Unit coverage: 97.6 percent of statements in src/engine, 20.0 percent across all of src (UI is covered by Playwright only).
  • npm audit: 12 issues in all dependencies (2 critical, 5 high, 5 moderate), 2 moderate in production dependencies (react-router).
  • Bundle: one 696 KB JavaScript file (142 KB gzip) that includes all 11 locale files (337 KB of JSON).
  • Lighthouse mobile (simulated slow 4G, 4x CPU): landing 94 performance, 100 accessibility, 100 best practices, 91 SEO; picker 96, 100, 100, 91.

Screens

Walked on 2026-10-20 against the production build (npx vite preview, port 4180) for Thabo, Lerato and Ayanda: landing, picker, consent, Home, Plan, Money, import with the sample CSV, Inbox, Privacy, Me. Screens are in docs/review/screens/ named <persona>-<page>-<theme>-<width>.png. These are observations for the panel to weigh, not conclusions.

Flow and friction points

  1. Three steps before any value. Landing, then the picker, then a consent screen with five notice cards and three switches, then Home. On a 390px phone the consent screen is several screens tall before "Agree and continue". Evidence: thabo-consent-dark-390.png.
  2. Short but "safe to spend R310". Ayanda is shown as Short from 23 Oct, two days before her next money, yet the stats card says R310 is safe to spend today, while her usual daily spend is R623. The two figures come from different rules (runway versus balance spread to the latest next money) and are not reconciled on screen. Evidence: persona-snapshot.json (ayanda), ayanda-home-dark-1280.png.
  3. Headline and chart can disagree. For Ayanda the hero says Short, while the 30 day chart's readout for today says Enough, because the chart assumes income lands as usual and the headline does not. It is labelled "If money lands as usual" in small text. Evidence: ayanda-home-light-390.png.
  4. Pre-filled what-if. Plan opens with a R500 expense already entered, so the result panel shows a change ("days less") before the user has done anything. Evidence: thabo-plan-dark-1280.png.
  5. Languages show English. Choosing any of the 10 other languages keeps English text everywhere, with a small note. A judge choosing isiZulu sees no change. Evidence: src/i18n/status.json (all "pending"), picker screenshots.
  6. Starting state on the picker is fixed. Persona cards always show the designed starting state (Tight, Enough, Short) even after an import or edit changes it. Evidence: src/pages/Picker.tsx uses p.startState.
  7. Edited seed data freezes. Once a seed transaction is corrected, the record stops refreshing daily, so on later days the history no longer ends yesterday and the 60 day spend window drifts. Evidence: src/data/localRepository.ts getRecord (only source === 'seed' refreshes).
  8. Privacy is one tap further on phones. The bottom bar has Home, Plan, Money, Inbox and Me; Privacy is the shield icon in the top bar. Evidence: src/layouts/nav.ts, any *-390.png.
  9. Sample import changes the person. "Use sample statement" replaces the active persona's history with a different, fictional domestic worker's statement, so Thabo becomes someone else with Thabo's name. Evidence: src/lib/sampleStatement.ts, thabo-import-sample-*.png.
  10. What helps most can suggest moving a car instalment. For Thabo, the bill-move idea names the car instalment, which in real life may carry penalties. Evidence: persona-snapshot.json (thabo help.billToMove).
  11. Human review goes nowhere. "Question this result" stores a request locally and confirms "Request received" with a reference. It is labelled simulated, but a user could believe a person will reply. Evidence: src/pages/privacy/Review.tsx, thabo-privacy-review-*.png.
  12. Things that worked smoothly. Persona switch from the sidebar, Ctrl K menu, theme switching with no flash, CSV sample import with skipped-row count, export of JSON and CSV, delete with receipt, offline reload (Playwright flow test).

4.1Retail banking product lead

Reviewer file: docs/review/product-lead.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Simulated perspective. I am not FNB staff and know nothing of FNB's internal policy, systems, roadmap or pricing. Everything about banks below comes from public pages cited in Sources.

Verdict

Tengo has one idea a bank app does not clearly offer today: a date-first answer ("Enough until Tue 27 Oct") built on how irregular income actually arrives, with a range and a plain state. That is a real gap next to what is public: FNB's nav» Money does categorised spend, smart budgets and alerts and reports five million users; Standard Bank's Future Payments add-on already forecasts debits 14 and 30 days ahead; Standard Bank's Budget Manager pre-fills a budget; Capitec auto-categorises spend; Absa publicises a Savings Coach. None of those, as publicly described, predicts when income lands or turns that into a run-out date. But as a bank product it is not pilot-ready. The engine ignores the things a bank knows best and that matter most to this segment (overdraft and credit limits, scheduled debit orders, multiple accounts, pending card holds), the headline mixes signals ("Enough until" next to a Tight chip), the Plan page uses credit-adjacent language ("Safe to lend up to") that a bank's compliance team would not ship as is, and there is no USSD or low-data path for the customers it claims to serve. The best route is not a separate app but a single "until when" card inside an existing money-management area, fed by internal transaction data, piloted with a narrow segment whose income is paid into the account (e-hailing and platform earners) before anyone whose income is mostly cash.

What works

  • A crisp, distinct core answer. The Home hero leads with one date, a state word, an icon and a three-segment gauge, then a range and "Next money around". Evidence: docs/review/screens/thabo-home-dark-1280.png, docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-dark-390.png, src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:9.
  • Income cadence is modelled, not assumed monthly. Weekday patterns for daily earners and gap percentiles for lumpy earners give an earliest, typical and latest next money. That is the piece salaried-oriented bank budgeting tools do not publicly describe. Evidence: docs/review/prep/fact-sheet.md (Forecast engine), src/engine/forecast.ts:103, docs/review/prep/persona-snapshot.json (pattern: weekday versus interval).
  • Transparent and explainable. The "Why this date" drawer lists every input, which suits a bank's need to explain automated outputs to customers and complaints teams. Evidence: docs/review/screens/thabo-why-drawer-dark-1280.png, README "How the forecast works, in plain words".
  • Integration-friendly seams. All data access sits behind TengoRepository and messages behind MessageWriter, so a bank could swap in an internal data feed and its own notification service without touching UI. Evidence: src/data/repository.ts, src/data/index.ts, README "What is simulated".
  • Engine is small, pure and tested. 97.6 percent statement coverage in src/engine, which lowers the cost of a bank team re-implementing or validating it server side. Evidence: docs/review/prep/fact-sheet.md (Quality signals), docs/review/prep/coverage.txt.
  • Daily message is privacy-aware by design. The preview promises only the date, cover and safe-to-spend figure, never balance or transactions. Evidence: docs/review/screens/thabo-inbox-dark-390.png.

What to improve

PROD-01. The engine ignores the bank data that defines this customer's real position. - Severity: High - Evidence: zeroDate treats zero as the end of money (src/engine/forecast.ts:196 to 210, "if (bal <= 0) return date"). There is no input for an overdraft or credit facility limit, scheduled debit orders or future-dated payments, pending card authorisations, or more than one account. Bills are inferred from history only (same description, within 20 percent, 24 to 36 days apart, at least twice: src/engine/classify.ts:16), so a new debit order or an annual premium is invisible. FNB publicly offers app-based overdraft applications (fnb.co.za overdraft page), and Standard Bank's Future Payments already predicts upcoming debits from recurring payments. A bank customer with a R2 000 overdraft who is at R0 is not "out of money" in the bank's view, and a customer with a debit order next week that has only run once is at risk Tengo cannot see. - Recommendation: Define a bank data contract for the engine: available balance versus ledger balance, facility limit and whether to count it (default off, shown separately as "then you would be using your overdraft"), scheduled debit orders and future-dated payments as known bills, and an account set. Keep inferred bills as a fallback. - Effort: M

PROD-02. Overlap with existing bank tools is not addressed, so the proposition reads as "another budgeting screen". - Severity: High - Evidence: FNB nav» Money: auto-categorised spend, smart budgets with alerts, credit status, saving goals, five million users and 1.1 million smart budgets (ITWeb, March 2025). Standard Bank: Budget Manager, Money Movements and Future Payments with 14 and 30 day predicted debits (Standard Bank pages). Capitec: automatic spend categorisation (Capitec blog). Tengo's Money page repeats categorisation and a recurring bills list (docs/review/screens/lerato-money-dark-1280.png), and the spending breakdown is keyword-based (src/engine/spending.ts), which is weaker than a bank's merchant-coded categories. Nothing in the app or README positions it against these. - Recommendation: Narrow the bank pitch to the one thing that is new: an income-aware run-out date and safe-to-spend figure for irregular earners. Drop or de-emphasise categorisation in the bank version and show how the date card would sit beside an existing budgeting tool. Add a one-page "what a bank already has versus what Tengo adds" slide. - Effort: S

PROD-03. Headline signals conflict, which in a bank app means complaints and call-centre volume. - Severity: High - Evidence: Thabo shows "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" with a Tight chip: the label is "Enough until" for every non-Short state (src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:9, docs/review/screens/thabo-home-dark-1280.png). Ayanda is Short from Fri 23 Oct yet "Safe to spend today R310", while her usual daily spend is R623, and the 30 day chart readout for today says Enough (docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-dark-390.png, docs/review/prep/persona-snapshot.json). The same "Enough until" plus Tight pairing appears in Plan (docs/review/screens/thabo-plan-dark-1280.png, src/pages/Plan.tsx:44). - Recommendation: Use a neutral label ("Lasts until" or "Covered until") for all states, and make the safe-to-spend figure obey the state (for Short, show the gap or the cut needed rather than a positive spend figure). Make the chart readout say "if money lands as usual" next to the state, not in small print. - Effort: S

PROD-04. Credit-adjacent and advice-like copy would not pass a bank's brand and compliance review as written. - Severity: Medium - Evidence: Loan check headline "Safe to lend up to R320" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:523, docs/review/screens/thabo-plan-dark-1280.png). "What helps most" suggests paying the car instalment after its due date (docs/review/screens/thabo-home-dark-1280.png, persona snapshot help.billToMove: "Car instalment"), which for a bank that may hold that very loan is a direct conflict. "You could set aside up to" (en.json:702) edges towards a savings recommendation. Whether any of this is regulated advice is for the legal reviewer; my point is that a bank's own compliance and brand teams would block it by default. - Recommendation: Reword to descriptive outcomes ("If you lend R300, your date moves to Mon 26 Oct"), remove "safe" from lending, never suggest delaying a credit repayment, and add a short "This is an estimate, not advice" line on Plan. Prepare the copy set for a compliance review before any pilot. - Effort: S

PROD-05. No channel fit for the lowest-data customers it claims to serve. - Severity: Medium - Evidence: Tengo is a web PWA only (docs/review/prep/fact-sheet.md, Form). The daily message is previewed as WhatsApp or SMS but nothing is sent (docs/review/screens/thabo-inbox-dark-390.png), and the SMS text is the same six-line message, which is long for a single SMS. FNB already serves cellphone banking over USSD on 120321# with balances and mini statements (FNB cellphone banking page). Sending a safe-to-spend figure over WhatsApp also puts financial information on a third-party channel, which a bank would need to approve. - Recommendation: Design three bank channel formats: an in-app card (primary), a one-line USSD answer ("Lasts until Tue 27 Oct. Tight. Safe today R295") on an existing USSD menu, and a short push or SMS under 160 characters. Treat WhatsApp as optional and subject to the bank's channel policy. - Effort: M

PROD-06. No realistic external data path, so a pilot depends entirely on a bank sponsor. - Severity: Medium - Evidence: The only real data entry is CSV import (src/lib/csv.ts, src/pages/Import.tsx); PDF is "coming next" and there is no bank connection (en.json:472). Public commentary says South Africa's open finance framework had no formal developments in 2025 and adoption by the FSCA is "still on the cards" for 2026 (Moonstone). Asking an irregular earner to export and upload a CSV every week is not a viable retention loop. - Recommendation: Make the pilot ask explicit: internal transaction data, run server side by the bank, with an internal sponsor in the digital money-management or financial inclusion team. Pilot prerequisites to list in the pitch: a data contract (PROD-01), a server-side engine with backtest results on real anonymised data, a copy set cleared by compliance (PROD-04), a channel plan (PROD-05), success metrics (for example arrears and unpaid debit orders in the pilot group versus a control), and a kill criterion. - Effort: L

PROD-07. Segment fit is asserted, not shown, and the cash-heavy persona is the weakest fit for bank data. - Severity: Medium - Evidence: All three personas are synthetic composites (src/data/seed/personas.ts); there is no user research in the repo. Ayanda (spaza owner) earns "lumpy cash takings" (BRIEF.md section 11); cash spent before it is deposited never appears in a bank feed, so the forecast sees only net deposits. Thabo's "Ride app weekly payout" lands in the account (docs/review/screens/thabo-home-dark-1280.png), which is the case bank data handles best. - Recommendation: Lead the bank pilot with platform and gig earners whose income is paid into the account, then hairdressers and small traders who deposit regularly. Treat cash-first traders as a later phase that needs a cash-entry feature. Run 5 to 8 interviews per segment before a pilot. - Effort: M

PROD-08. Standalone onboarding and placeholders do not fit inside a bank app. - Severity: Low - Evidence: Three steps before value (landing, picker, consent with five cards and three switches) (docs/review/prep/walkthrough.md item 1, docs/review/screens/thabo-consent-dark-390.png). All 10 non-English locales are English text (src/i18n/status.json). Logo and icons are placeholders (README "Brand and type"). - Recommendation: For the bank version, design an in-app opt-in of one screen that reuses the bank's existing consent and terms, inherit the bank's design system, and get first-language review of at least isiZulu, Sesotho and Afrikaans before any customer sees them. - Effort: M

Top three risks

  1. Wrong date, real harm, bank's name on it. Inside a bank app, a confident "Enough until" that misses an overdraft, a new debit order or a late payout becomes the bank's error, with complaints and reputational cost (PROD-01, PROD-03).
  2. Absorbed as a feature. Banks already have budgeting, alerts and, at Standard Bank, a 14 and 30 day payment forecast. A bank could add income cadence to an existing tool without Tengo; the defensible part is small unless backed by proven accuracy on real data (PROD-02).
  3. No data, no pilot. With open finance not yet adopted and CSV as the only input, Tengo has no route to real users except a bank sponsor, and the current copy and channel gaps would slow that sponsor's internal approvals (PROD-04, PROD-05, PROD-06).

Score

5/10. A genuinely distinct date-first, income-aware answer with a clean explainable engine, but it ignores bank-side data like overdrafts and debit orders, mixes signals in the headline and is not yet shaped for a bank's compliance, channels or segments.

  • ITWeb, "FNB adds more functionality to nav platform" (smart budget, from search index): https://itweb.co.za/article/fnb-adds-more-functionality-to-nav-platform/DZQ58MVPaJOvzXy2 (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • FNB nav» Money page (Money Coach; direct fetch blocked by a browser check, content seen in search index only): https://www.fnb.co.za/ways-to-bank/digital/navMoney.html (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • FNB overdraft page (app application; direct fetch blocked, content from search index): https://www.fnb.co.za/for-you/borrow/overdraft.html (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • FNB cellphone banking (USSD 120321#; content from search index): https://www.fnb.co.za/ways-to-bank/cellphone-banking-index.html (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • Standard Bank, "Do more with our banking app" (Budget Manager, Future Payments 14 to 30 days, Money Movements, My360): https://www.standardbank.co.za/southafrica/personal/learn/do-more-with-our-banking-app-part-1 (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • Standard Bank, Future Payments add-on help page: https://standardbank.co.za/southafrica/personal/products-and-services/ways-to-bank/help-centre/future-payments-add-on (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • Capitec blog, "What if there was a simpler way to budget?" (automatic categorisation; direct fetch returned 403, content from search index): https://www.capitecbank.co.za/blog/articles/best-way-to-bank/what-if-there-was-a-simpler-way-to-budget/ (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • Absa Savings Coach: https://www.absa.co.za/offers/absa-savings-coach/ (accessed 2026-10-20, content from search index)
  • Moonstone, "Key fintech policy developments to watch in 2026" (open finance status): https://www.moonstone.co.za/key-fintech-policy-developments-to-watch-in-2026/ (accessed 2026-10-20)

4.2Head of technology and solution architect

Reviewer file: docs/review/technology.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

As a hackathon build, Tengo is well made. It has a pure, deterministic forecast engine with 97.6 percent statement coverage, clean layering, strict TypeScript, good scores on throttled mobile, and a small, permissively licensed set of dependencies. As the base for a real product it is a well-built client-only prototype, and the README's claim that it can be swapped to a backend without touching UI code is optimistic. The most serious technical defect is about correctness, not scale. Once a user imports or edits data, the record stops ageing. The engine then treats every elapsed day as zero spend against an unchanged balance, so the state drifts towards Enough while confidence stays "high". My benchmark shows Ayanda moving from Short to Enough after 30 days with no new data. The other gaps are expected in a demo but must close before a pilot. There is no observability at all. The repository contract is coarse and cannot be scoped to real users. localStorage writes are unguarded. The service worker caching is naive. The code is never split into smaller chunks.

What works

  • The engine is pure, deterministic and well tested. src/engine/forecast.ts and classify.ts are side-effect-free functions with named constants (forecast.ts:18-28, classify.ts:16-22). Statement coverage is 97.55 percent for src/engine and 98.59 percent for forecast.ts (docs/review/prep/coverage.txt). There are 66 unit tests across 9 files and all pass (docs/review/prep/checks.md).
  • Date arithmetic is safe across time zones. Calendar days are converted to UTC day numbers (src/engine/dates.ts:6-9), so a device in a different time zone cannot shift the forecast date.
  • The engine is fast enough at realistic volumes. forecast() runs in 0.36 ms on 285 transactions, 0.83 to 1.02 ms on about 2 000, and 2.23 ms on 10 080 (laptop, docs/review/tools/technology/engine-bench.out.txt, script engine-bench.ts).
  • Layering is clean at the edges. Zustand holds only UI preferences (src/store/session.ts:18). All persona data goes through one TengoRepository interface (src/data/repository.ts:75), and a single line chooses the implementation (src/data/index.ts:5).
  • It performs well on throttled mobile. Lighthouse mobile (slow 4G at 150 ms RTT, 4x CPU) gives landing 94 and picker 96 for performance, with TBT of 160 ms and 10 ms, LCP of 2.4 s and 2.3 s, CLS 0, and 218 KiB total (docs/review/prep/lighthouse/*.report.json). Home under the same throttling shows the hero in 1 516 ms cold and 627 ms warm, transferring 149 KB (docs/review/prep/perf-home.json).
  • Privacy is tested in code. A Playwright test asserts no third-party requests (tests/flows.spec.ts:117), and the service worker ignores other origins (public/sw.js:24).
  • Dependencies are few and permissively licensed. There are 10 production dependencies, licensed MIT (18), OFL-1.1 (2), ISC (1) and Apache-2.0 (1), with no copyleft (docs/review/prep/audit.md).
  • There is a quality toolchain. Lint, a dash checker, i18n key parity, font coverage and 116 contrast pairs are all scripted and pass (docs/review/prep/checks.md). A pre-paint script stops the theme from flashing (index.html:11-22).

What to improve

TECH-01 Imported or edited data never ages, so the forecast drifts towards Enough
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: src/data/localRepository.ts:87 refreshes only source === 'seed', so imported and edited records stay fixed. src/engine/forecast.ts:36-41 measures history up to today, not up to the last transaction, and forecast.ts:66 counts days with no transactions as zero spend. The balance is never reduced for days that have passed. Running the real engine on the seeded records viewed later with no new data (docs/review/tools/technology/engine-bench.out.txt): Thabo moves from Tight on 27 Oct to Enough on 13 Nov after 7 days. Ayanda moves from Short to Tight after 7 days and to Enough after 30, with central spend falling from R623 to R79 a day. Confidence stays "high" throughout. The import page's rebase (src/lib/csv.ts:160-162) fixes this only at the moment of import. The walkthrough (item 7) noted the freeze but not that it inflates the state.
  • Recommendation: Store an asOf date with the balance. Run the engine with today = asOf + 1, or subtract the estimated spend for the elapsed days, and measure the spend window up to the last transaction. Show "Your data is N days old". Lower the confidence after 3 days and hide the state after about 14 days until the user refreshes. Add a unit test for staleness.
  • Effort: S
TECH-02 No observability: errors, performance and forecast quality are invisible
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: The only error handling is console.error (src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx:17). A search for Sentry, onerror or unhandledrejection finds nothing. There is one boundary, at the root (src/App.tsx:22), so any render error replaces the whole app, which contradicts its own comment "one broken screen never blanks the whole app" (ErrorBoundary.tsx:8). Promise failures in writes are not caught at all: src/pages/Import.tsx:55-60 has no try/catch, and void calls in src/data/PersonaProvider.tsx:80 drop their errors. Nothing measures web vitals, and nothing records forecast outcomes against what actually happened, so accuracy can never be tracked in production.
  • Recommendation: Add error reporting with personal data scrubbed (no amounts, descriptions or names), route-level error boundaries, a global unhandledrejection handler, web-vitals sampling, and a privacy-safe forecast accuracy event (predicted state and date against the observed outcome, bucketed). Define SLOs before a pilot.
  • Effort: M
TECH-03 The repository contract is not ready for a real backend
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Keys are a closed union of three personas (src/data/seed/personas.ts:3), with no user, tenant or auth context in any method (src/data/repository.ts:75-95). Every read and write parses and rewrites one JSON blob holding all personas (src/data/localRepository.ts:57-69). getRecord returns the full history and replaceHistory sends it all back: about 183 KB of JSON for 2 000 transactions (engine-bench.out.txt). There is no paging, no incremental sync and no version or ETag for concurrent edits. Each write makes 2 or 3 sequential calls: setConsent then appendAudit then getAudit (PersonaProvider.tsx:112-117). The "audit log" is written by the client, can be edited by the user and is cut to 200 entries (localRepository.ts:16,146). Pages also bypass the provider and call the repository directly (src/pages/Me.tsx:26,32, src/pages/Picker.tsx:35,40, src/components/CommandPalette.tsx:92).
  • Recommendation: Redesign the contract for a hosted service: user-scoped methods that take an auth context, paged and incremental transaction sync (cursor or since), optimistic concurrency, write endpoints that return the audit entry they created, and a server-side append-only audit trail with no client delete. Move the engine into a shared package so the server can produce authoritative forecasts and alerts while the client keeps offline what-ifs.
  • Effort: L
TECH-04 localStorage writes are unguarded and the storage is fragile
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: write() calls setItem with no try/catch (src/data/localRepository.ts:67-69). A QuotaExceededError therefore rejects replaceHistory. Import.tsx:57-58 then leaves saving set to true and never navigates. MDN gives a limit of 5 MiB per origin and says Safari deletes script-written data after seven days without interaction (source below). 10 080 transactions take 897 KB of JSON, which is about 1.8 MB in browsers that store UTF-16 (engine-bench.out.txt). There is no storage event listener, so two open tabs overwrite each other's blob (last writer wins). Corrupt data is silently replaced with an empty store (localRepository.ts:61-64), which loses consent and audit records without telling the user.
  • Recommendation: Wrap writes and show a clear error. Move records to IndexedDB, request navigator.storage.persist(), listen for storage events or use a BroadcastChannel, and keep a backup copy before overwriting corrupt data. Before any pilot, add server sync so clearing the browser does not lose a user's history.
  • Effort: M
TECH-05 The service worker caching strategy is naive
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: The worker is registered on load (src/main.tsx:16-19), so the first visit's JS and CSS are fetched before it controls the page. Offline works only after a second load, and the test reloads before going offline (tests/flows.spec.ts:131-140). Every same-origin GET that is not a navigation is cache-first under a fixed name tengo-v2 (public/sw.js:4,40-52). As a result manifest.webmanifest and the icons never refresh, and old hashed bundles pile up forever because the cache name never changes with the build. Navigations write any response, including a 404 or 5xx, over the cached / (sw.js:27-35). vercel.json sends every unmatched path to index.html, so a missing old asset comes back as HTML with status 200 and gets cached under a .js URL.
  • Recommendation: Generate a precache manifest from the build (Workbox or vite-plugin-pwa) and precache it at install. Version the cache by build hash and clean up old entries. Cache only res.ok responses with the expected content type. Use stale-while-revalidate for files without a hash. Exclude /assets/* from the SPA rewrite.
  • Effort: S
TECH-06 One 696 KB bundle with every locale loaded at start
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: There is a single index-*.js of 695 602 bytes raw, 141 864 gzip (docs/review/prep/audit.md), and Vite warns that a chunk is over 500 kB (checks.md). Locales are imported eagerly (src/i18n/index.ts:5), as are all pages (src/App.tsx:8-18) and the seed generator. Lighthouse estimates 41 to 47 KiB of unused JS. Today the 11 locale files cost little because 10 are copies of English: 12.8 KB gzip together. Compressed one at a time they total 104 KB gzip (measured with gzip -c per file on src/i18n/locales). Assumption: real translations compress about like separate files, so translating adds roughly 80 to 100 KB gzip to the first load, about 60 to 70 percent more JS on 2G or 3G for users who pay for data.
  • Recommendation: Load English plus the chosen language on demand and let the service worker prefetch the rest when idle, so switching language offline still works. Lazy-load routes (Plan, Money, Import, Privacy, Inbox). Keep the seed generator out of the main chunk. Set a bundle budget in CI.
  • Effort: S
TECH-07 Test strategy leaves the UI, data layer and failure paths unproven
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: Statement coverage is 19.95 percent for all of src, with 0 percent for every component and page, and 0 percent for PersonaProvider.tsx (coverage.txt). 136 of the 221 Playwright tests are screenshot runs whose only assertion is no horizontal overflow, with no visual baseline (tests/screens.spec.ts:96-98). Playwright uses Chromium only (playwright.config.ts:13), so iOS Safari, where storage eviction applies, is untested. There is no CI configuration (no .github folder). Nothing tests quota errors, stale data (TECH-01), multiple tabs, time zones or service worker updates. localRepository.test.ts has 5 tests.
  • Recommendation: Add CI running build, unit tests, lint, audit and Playwright on every push. Add a WebKit project and a low-end Android emulation profile. Use toHaveScreenshot baselines for the key screens. Add component tests for PersonaProvider and Import. Add engine property tests (balance monotonicity, state ordering, staleness).
  • Effort: M
TECH-08 Known advisories in shipped and build dependencies
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: react-router-dom 6.30 is affected by GHSA-wrjc-x8rr-h8h6, an open redirect through <Link> and useNavigate (moderate, patched in 7.18.0), and by GHSA-337j-9hxr-rhxg, which concerns SSR hydration (audit.md). The app passes only internal constant paths or an encoded receipt id to navigate (src/pages/privacy/Delete.tsx:21, CommandPalette.tsx:82), so practical exposure is low, and Tengo has no SSR. The dev toolchain has 2 critical advisories (tinypool, through vitest 3) and 5 high (braces, through tailwind 3 and chokidar), all dev-only but a risk on build machines.
  • Recommendation: Upgrade to react-router 7.18 or later in library mode, and to vitest 5 and tailwind 4 (or pin fixed transitive versions with npm overrides). Add npm audit --omit=dev as a CI gate and use Renovate or Dependabot.
  • Effort: M
TECH-09 Engine runs on the main thread and is repeated many times per render
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: helpPlan runs forecast inside a binary search over R10 steps up to R200 000 (src/engine/help.ts:6,29-39), then again for each candidate bill. It is called on every Home render where the record changes (src/components/forecast/HelpCard.tsx:17). Measured on a laptop: 4.2 ms at 285 transactions, 10 to 12 ms at about 2 000, 40 ms at 10 080 (engine-bench.out.txt). loanCheck and whatIf run again on every keystroke (src/pages/Plan.tsx:65-73,190). Assumption: a low-end Android is 4 to 10 times slower than the laptop, giving 40 to 120 ms at 2 000 transactions and 160 to 400 ms at 10 000. That is noticeable input lag on Home and Plan.
  • Recommendation: Debounce the Plan inputs. Cache the base forecast by record version. Solve extraNeeded in closed form from the runway (it is linear in balance) instead of searching. Move heavy work to a Web Worker if histories grow.
  • Effort: S
TECH-10 The fake 140 ms latency ships to production and is applied unevenly
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: new LocalRepository(window.localStorage, 140) in production (src/data/index.ts:5). It adds 140 ms to every cold Home load (perf-home.json, hero at 1 516 ms). It applies to getRecord, getConsent, getAudit and deletePersona, but not to setConsent, appendAudit, getReviewRequests or getDeletionReceipts (localRepository.ts:129-163,182). The loading states therefore do not model a real network.
  • Recommendation: Make the latency a development-only flag, and test loading and error states with Playwright route delays and failures instead.
  • Effort: S
TECH-11 "Today" is fixed when the app opens
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: const [today] = useState(() => localToday()) (src/data/PersonaProvider.tsx:36). An installed PWA left open overnight keeps showing yesterday's forecast and does not trigger the daily seed refresh.
  • Recommendation: Recompute today on visibilitychange and at local midnight, and reload when it changes.
  • Effort: S
TECH-12 No security or caching headers in the hosting config
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: vercel.json contains only an SPA rewrite: no Content-Security-Policy, no frame-ancestors, no Permissions-Policy, and no immutable cache header for hashed /assets/*. For a money app that will later hold bank data, the CSP is the main defence against injected scripts reading localStorage.
  • Recommendation: Add a strict CSP (default-src 'self', with a hash for the inline theme script at index.html:11), frame-ancestors 'none', Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy, long immutable caching for /assets/* and no-cache for /sw.js.
  • Effort: S

What breaks first at 100 000 users

Serving the app is not the problem: it is a static site on a CDN. What breaks first, in order:

  1. Trust, through stale and lost data. Users who imported once see drifting forecasts (TECH-01). Safari users who stay away for a week lose everything (TECH-04).
  2. The team's ability to see any of it. No telemetry means the first signal is a support ticket or a social media post (TECH-02).
  3. The repository when a backend arrives. Full-history payloads and 2 or 3 calls per write (TECH-03) multiply load. Assumption: with 30 percent daily active users each making about 6 data calls a day, that is about 180 000 calls a day, which is trivial for a managed Postgres service. The real costs are bank-feed ingestion, key management and the audit store, none of which exist yet.

Top three risks

  1. The forecast misleads users as data ages (TECH-01). A frozen import shows Enough to someone who is Short, with high confidence. That is a product safety risk, not only a bug.
  2. Running blind (TECH-02). Without error, performance or accuracy telemetry, a pilot cannot show the forecast works or catch regressions on low-end phones.
  3. Underestimating the backend (TECH-03, TECH-04). The repository swap is presented as easy. In practice auth, user scoping, sync, a server-side audit trail and data durability are most of the work for a pilot.

Score

6 out of 10. The engine and front end are clean, fast and well tested for a demo, but data freshness, observability and the backend contract all need real work before a pilot.

Sources

  • MDN, "Storage quotas and eviction criteria": https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • GitHub Advisory GHSA-wrjc-x8rr-h8h6, React Router open redirect: https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wrjc-x8rr-h8h6 (accessed 2026-10-20)

4.3Security and privacy lead (POPIA)

Reviewer file: docs/review/security-privacy.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

Tengo's privacy centre is a thoughtful demonstration of data subject rights for a hackathon: consent, access, export, correction, human review, a sample breach notice and deletion all exist as working screens, nothing leaves the browser, and a test checks there are no third party requests. As a model of what a real deployment would do, it is weaker than it looks. Several on-screen statements are written as facts but are not implemented: the "Kept for 12 months, then removed" retention rule is only a computed date and never deletes anything, the "Never uses account numbers or who you pay" panel is contradicted by storing statement descriptions verbatim (the bundled sample already holds "Client payment, Mrs K"), and green ticks sit next to encryption and access claims. Deletion removes the persona slot but leaves a receipt that proves nothing, and the audit log stores transaction descriptions, including the value a user later corrected. None of this harms anyone today because the data is fictional and local. It would matter the moment a real statement is imported, and nothing yet exists for authentication, server-side storage, key management, operator contracts, cross-border transfer, breach handling or a registered Information Officer. This is a simulated professional view based on the code and public sources, not legal advice.

What works

  • Everything is local and same-origin. The service worker only handles same-origin GETs (public/sw.js:23) and a Playwright test asserts no third party requests (tests/flows.spec.ts:117). The README states no analytics scripts (README.md:104).
  • Clear "simulated" labelling on the consent screen and privacy centre: "Simulated privacy controls. Not a compliance certification." (src/pages/Consent.tsx:64-66, src/pages/privacy/Overview.tsx:55-57, screenshot docs/review/screens/thabo-consent-light-390.png). The Information Officer card is flagged "Placeholder details. Not a real contact." (Overview.tsx:157).
  • Rights are mapped to real POPIA concepts: access and export (src/pages/privacy/MyData.tsx:27-48), correction routed to Money (privacy.correctHint), a human review route that echoes the representation right for automated decisions in section 71 (src/pages/privacy/Review.tsx), complaint to the Information Regulator (privacy.complaints), and a breach notice template with the right sections (src/pages/privacy/Breach.tsx, stamped "Sample").
  • Withdrawing core consent pauses the forecast screens while leaving Privacy and Me open, so a person can still see and delete data (src/layouts/AppGate.tsx:9,35). Analytics is off by default (Consent.tsx:28).
  • CSV import drops every column except date, description, amount and balance (src/lib/csv.ts:130-152) and the file is read in the browser, never uploaded (src/pages/Import.tsx:99). That is genuine minimisation for column-level data.
  • Deletion is a two-step confirm with an explicit checkbox (src/pages/privacy/Delete.tsx:46-66) and does remove transactions, balance, consent, audit and review requests for that persona (src/data/localRepository.ts:165-180, confirmed by docs/review/tools/security-privacy/deletion-probe.ts).
  • The repository interface (src/data/repository.ts) gives one seam where server-side access control, encryption and audit can be added without touching UI code.
  • No dangerouslySetInnerHTML, innerHTML or eval in src (grep, 2026-10-20). React escaping covers the i18n escapeValue: false setting (src/i18n/index.ts:14) because no translated string reaches an HTML sink.

What to improve

SEC-01 Retention rule is displayed as fact but never enforced

Severity: High Evidence: The privacy centre says "Kept for 12 months, then removed." and "Next removal {{date}}" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:234, docs/review/screens/thabo-privacy-dark-1280.png), the consent notice says "12 months, then it is removed" (consent.notice.how.body), and the README says retention "works against local storage" (README.md:94). src/pages/privacy/retention.ts:6-11 only computes a date; the only caller is the display (Overview.tsx:40). Probe: a record dated 2024-01-05 is still held on 2026-10-20 (docs/review/tools/security-privacy/deletion-probe.ts, output "2024 record still held after 12+ months: true"). Seed data hides this because it is 90 days old and regenerated daily. POPIA section 14(4) requires destruction or de-identification as soon as reasonably practicable once authority to keep ends, and 14(5) requires that destruction prevents reconstruction. Recommendation: Either implement a purge (drop transactions older than the window on every read and log it) or change the copy to "In a real service, records older than 12 months would be removed". In production, run a scheduled server job, cover backups and operator copies, and record the run. Effort: S

SEC-02 On-screen wording reads as real security and compliance claims

Severity: High Evidence: "How data is protected" shows green check icons (Overview.tsx:105-109) beside "In a real deployment, data is encrypted in transit and at rest." and "Access is limited to you. Staff access would be logged and reviewed." (en.json:205,207). There is no real deployment, so the tick asserts a control that does not exist. Delete says "I understand this removes the data for good" (en.json:330) and "A deletion receipt is kept" (en.json:327). The human review says a person would "reply within 5 working days" (en.json:288). The breach sample says "told the Information Regulator" (privacy.breach.doing.body). One banner at the top is easy to crop out of a screenshot or pitch slide (docs/review/screens/thabo-privacy-dark-1280.png). Recommendation: Replace ticks with a neutral "planned" icon, write every future control in the conditional ("would be"), put "Demo" on each card that describes a future control, and keep the banner on every privacy sub-page. Do not quote these screens as evidence of compliance in submission material. Effort: S

SEC-03 "Never uses names, account numbers or who you pay" is false for real statements

Severity: High Evidence: The panel lists "Your name", "Account or card numbers" and "Who you pay, beyond the description" under "Never uses" (en.json:188-201). Import keeps the description verbatim (src/lib/csv.ts:133,163) and it is stored and exported unchanged (MyData.tsx:44). Bank statement descriptions routinely hold payee names, references and partial account numbers (Assumption, from typical South African statement layouts; likely in most debit-order and transfer rows). The project's own sample contains third party names: "Client payment, Mrs K" and "Client payment, Waterkloof" (public/sample-statement.csv:2,7). That is personal information of third parties, who receive no notice. Financial history is personal information under the POPIA section 1 definition, item (b). Recommendation: Scrub descriptions on import (mask digit runs of 6 or more, South African ID number patterns, email and phone numbers), store a normalised merchant token instead of free text where possible, and change the copy to "Descriptions may contain names. We mask numbers." Add unit tests for the scrubber. Effort: M

SEC-04 No authentication, unencrypted financial data in localStorage, no security headers

Severity: High (for any real data), Low (for the demo) Evidence: All records, balance, consent, audit and reviews sit in plaintext JSON under tengo.data.v1 (localRepository.ts:15,67-69; src/data/index.ts:5). Anyone with the phone, any script on the origin, or a malicious browser extension can read it. Persona switching has no lock (src/pages/Picker.tsx:40-41), which on a shared phone means one person can open another's data. vercel.json sets only a rewrite: no Content-Security-Policy, HSTS, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy or Permissions-Policy. The inline theme script in index.html:11-22 means a strict CSP would need a hash. react-router 6.x carries a moderate open-redirect advisory (GHSA-wrjc-x8rr-h8h6, docs/review/prep/audit.md:45-47). POPIA section 19 requires appropriate, reasonable technical and organisational measures with regard to generally accepted practice. Recommendation: For real data: server-side storage with authentication (bank SSO or phone OTP plus device binding), an app PIN or biometric lock, short-lived tokens in memory or HttpOnly cookies rather than localStorage, and at most a minimal encrypted cache on device (WebCrypto key, non-extractable). Ship CSP with a script hash, HSTS and the other headers in vercel.json. Upgrade react-router. Effort: L

SEC-05 Deletion is local only and the receipt proves nothing

Severity: Medium Evidence: deletePersona deletes the slot then appends a receipt with personaId, timestamp and counts (localRepository.ts:165-180). Probe output after delete: {"personas":{},"receipts":[{"id":"DEL_MVGIYDW5GV114F","personaId":"thabo",...}]}. The receipt id comes from Math.random and Date.now (localRepository.ts:37-40), is unsigned, is stored on the same device whose data was "deleted", and survives forever until "Reset" (resetAll, line 186). The id is placed in the URL (Delete.tsx:21), so it lands in browser history. Review request count is not recorded in the receipt. Things deletion cannot reach: downloaded JSON and CSV exports (MyData.tsx:39,45), the original statement file, and in a real build any server copies, backups, logs and operator copies. Reopening the persona regenerates a fresh seed record immediately (probe: "source: seed tx: 220"), which can look like the deletion failed. Recommendation: In production, deletion is a server workflow: delete primary rows, tombstone for backup restore, purge from operators with confirmation, expire backups within a stated window, then issue a receipt signed by the server that holds no personal data beyond a pseudonymous id. Tell the user plainly what cannot be deleted (their own downloads). Keep the deletion record itself under a documented retention period. In the demo, drop personaId from the URL and say "receipt on this device only". Effort: M (demo copy S, real workflow L)

SEC-06 Consent is bundled, partly pre-ticked, and not provable

Severity: Medium Evidence: Core processing is a disabled toggle labelled "Required" (Consent.tsx:86), so "consent" for it is not voluntary in the sense of the POPIA section 1 definition ("voluntary, specific and informed"). Contract (section 11(1)(b)) is the more honest lawful basis for the core forecast. Daily messages default to on (Consent.tsx:27, screenshot thabo-consent-light-390.png). The ConsentRecord is overwritten on each change (localRepository.ts:129-135) and holds only booleans and a time: no notice version, language, channel or text shown. The only history is the audit log, capped at 200 entries (localRepository.ts:16,146) and erased on delete. Section 11(2)(a) puts the burden of proving consent on the responsible party. If messages ever carry offers, section 69 requires prior opt-in for electronic direct marketing. Recommendation: Base core processing on contract and say so; keep consent for messages and analytics, both off by default. Store append-only consent events with notice version hash, locale, UI surface and timestamp on the server, retained beyond profile deletion as proof under a documented period. Separate service messages from any marketing. Effort: M

SEC-07 Audit log holds personal data, keeps pre-correction values, and is not tamper-evident

Severity: Medium Evidence: Editing a transaction logs { name: tx.description } (src/components/TransactionEditor.tsx:41), so the original description survives in the log after the user corrects it (probe output {"name":"PAYMENT TO J SMITH","fields":"category"}). This undermines correction under section 24. Translated labels are stored as params (Overview.tsx:47). The log is client-writable, trimmed to the last 200 entries (localRepository.ts:146), and every page view adds session.opened and data.viewed (PersonaProvider.tsx:77-81, MyData.tsx:20-24), so a busy user loses older evidence quickly. Recommendation: Log event type, actor id, pseudonymous record id and changed field names only, never descriptions or amounts. In production write audit events server-side to append-only storage with hash chaining or WORM retention, separate from the user profile, and apply the same "no personal data in logs" rule to application logs, error tracking and analytics. Effort: M

SEC-08 Collection notice misses items section 18 requires

Severity: Medium Evidence: The consent notice covers what, why, who, where and how long (Consent.tsx:14-20, en.json:146-157). Section 18(1) also needs the responsible party's name and address, whether supply is voluntary or mandatory and the consequences, recipients or categories of recipients, the right to object, the right to complain to the Regulator with contact details, and any intended cross-border transfer. The complaint line exists only on the privacy page (privacy.complaints). The Information Officer is "To be appointed" with privacy@example.org (Overview.tsx:150-154); section 55(2) and the Regulator's guidance note say officers take up duties only after registration on the eServices portal. Recommendation: Add the missing items to a short layered notice with a link to a full privacy policy. Name the legal entity. Appoint and register an Information Officer before any pilot. Publish a PAIA manual. Effort: S (copy), M (entity, officer, manual)

SEC-09 Service worker would cache personal data once an API exists

Severity: Medium Evidence: Every same-origin GET that is not a navigation is served cache-first and cached on success (public/sw.js:38-49). Today that is only hashed assets. If the planned hosted repository (README.md:95) uses a same-origin API path or a proxy, account and transaction responses would be written to Cache Storage, survive logout and deletion, and be served stale to the next person on a shared phone. Navigations are cached as / (sw.js:30), harmless now, risky if HTML ever embeds user data. Recommendation: Allow-list cacheable paths (hashed /assets/*, icons, manifest) and make everything else network-only. Clear all caches and storage on logout and on deletion. Set Cache-Control: no-store on API responses. Effort: S

SEC-10 No plan for operators and cross-border transfer

Severity: Medium Evidence: The cross-border card says "nothing is sent to a server, inside or outside South Africa" (en.json:238). The README invites swapping in Supabase (README.md:95) and an LLM-backed MessageWriter (README.md:96), and the Inbox previews WhatsApp and SMS delivery. Each would be an operator under POPIA section 1, requiring a written contract obliging section 19 safeguards and immediate breach notice to the responsible party (section 21). A database region, LLM provider or messaging platform outside South Africa triggers section 72: transfer only with adequate protection by law, binding rules or agreement, consent, or contractual necessity. Message text containing a cover date and amount is financial personal information; sending it to an LLM or WhatsApp sends it to that provider. Recommendation: Keep a register of operators with region, data categories and contract status. Prefer a South African hosting region. For an LLM, send only templated, de-identified fields (no names, no descriptions) and contract for zero retention and no training. For WhatsApp or SMS, keep content minimal ("Your Tengo update is ready") to limit what lock screens and the platform see. Update the section 18 notice with transfers. Effort: M

SEC-11 Children and vulnerable users are not considered

Severity: Medium Evidence: There is no age question or gate anywhere (grep for "age" checks in src/pages, none). POPIA defines a child as under 18 (section 1) and section 34 prohibits processing a child's personal information unless a section 35 exception applies, such as prior consent from a competent person. The target users (informal earners, shared phones per the brief) include people at risk of financial control by a partner or family member; the app has no lock, shows full history on open, and the planned daily messages could reveal a balance on a shared or watched phone. Recommendation: Require 18 or older at onboarding (or a bank-verified adult account), add an app lock and a discreet notification mode, and run a short risk assessment for financial abuse and coercion with an inclusion partner. Effort: M

SEC-12 No breach response capability behind the sample notice

Severity: Medium Evidence: Breach.tsx shows a template only. There is no incident runbook, detection, contact list or log source. Since 1 April 2025 section 22 notifications to the Regulator must go through the eServices portal, and commentary reports no low-risk exemption. An operator must tell the responsible party immediately (section 21(2)). Recommendation: Before a pilot: an incident response plan with roles, a decision log, the SCN1 portal process, a data subject notice in all supported languages, operator notification clauses, and a tabletop exercise. Effort: M

SEC-13 Withdrawn consent does not stop processing

Severity: Low Evidence: With core consent off, AppGate hides forecast pages (AppGate.tsx:35) but PersonaProvider still computes the full forecast on every load (PersonaProvider.tsx:83-86) and data stays held. Section 11(2)(b) allows withdrawal at any time. Recommendation: Skip the forecast computation when consent.core is false, and offer deletion on the paused screen. On a server, stop scheduled jobs and messaging for that user. Effort: S

SEC-14 Export and import edge cases

Severity: Low Evidence: toCsv quotes only commas, quotes and newlines (src/lib/csv.ts:189-199); a description beginning with =, +, - or @ from an imported statement is written as a live spreadsheet formula (CSV injection). The JSON export includes the full audit log and free-text review details (MyData.tsx:28-38) as an unencrypted file in Downloads, with a note saying "Not real personal information", which would be false on real data. Import has no size limit (Import.tsx:99) and write does not catch QuotaExceededError (localRepository.ts:67-69), so a large file fails silently mid-flow. Recommendation: Prefix risky leading characters with a single quote in exported cells. Warn before export that the file is unprotected. Cap import size (for example 2 MB) and handle storage errors with a message. Effort: S

Threat model for a real deployment
Area Threat What must be built
Authentication Account takeover via SIM swap or shared phone; persona-style switching without a lock Bank SSO or OTP plus device binding, app PIN or biometric, session timeout, step-up for export and delete
Data in transit Interception, downgrade TLS 1.2 or higher, HSTS preload, certificate monitoring, no data in URLs (fix ?deleted= pattern)
Data at rest Database or backup leak, stolen phone Encrypted database and backups with KMS-managed keys, field-level encryption for descriptions and balance, minimal device cache, encrypted if kept
Row-level security One user reading another's rows through a client SDK RLS policies keyed to the authenticated user id on every table, deny by default, tests that try cross-user reads, no service-role key in the client
Key management Key leak in code or CI Keys in a managed KMS or secret store, rotation schedule, separate keys per environment, no keys in the bundle
Logging Personal data in app logs, error tracker, analytics Structured logs with ids only, scrubbers in the error SDK, retention limits, access to logs logged
Consent records Cannot prove consent Append-only consent events with notice version and locale (SEC-06)
Retention and deletion Data kept forever; deletion misses backups and operators Scheduled purge, backup expiry window, operator purge confirmation, signed receipt (SEC-01, SEC-05)
Breach response Late or missed section 22 notice Runbook, eServices SCN1 process, operator clauses (SEC-12)
Operators Processor misuse or breach Written contracts under section 21, register, due diligence (SEC-10)
Cross-border Transfer without basis to cloud, LLM or messaging provider Section 72 assessment, South African region where possible, de-identified LLM inputs (SEC-10)
Automated decisions A forecast or loan check that substantially affects the user without recourse Real staffed human review with logic explanation, as section 71 contemplates
Children and vulnerable users Minors, coerced users Age gate, discreet mode, lock (SEC-11)
Client side XSS stealing tokens or data, stale caches CSP, no tokens in localStorage, service worker allow-list (SEC-04, SEC-09)
Must exist before real user data is touched
  1. A named legal entity as responsible party, a registered Information Officer, a privacy policy and a PAIA manual.
  2. A documented lawful basis per purpose (contract for the forecast, consent for messages and analytics) and a section 18 notice with every required item.
  3. Authentication with device binding and an app lock; no personal data in localStorage beyond an encrypted, minimal cache.
  4. A hosted database with encryption at rest, row-level security tested against cross-user reads, KMS-managed keys and backups with a stated expiry.
  5. Description scrubbing on import (ID numbers, account numbers, phone numbers, emails) and an updated "what we use" panel that tells the truth.
  6. An enforced retention job and a deletion workflow that reaches backups and operators, with a server-signed receipt.
  7. Append-only consent and audit records with no transaction content in them.
  8. Security headers and CSP, service worker allow-list, dependency upgrades with no known high or critical issues in production dependencies.
  9. Written operator agreements and a section 72 assessment for every provider (database, LLM, WhatsApp or SMS, hosting, error tracking).
  10. An incident response plan, tested once, using the Regulator's eServices portal process.
  11. An age gate and a vulnerable-user risk assessment.
  12. A staffed human review process with a real response time before the screen promises one.
  13. An independent security review or penetration test, and a privacy impact assessment signed off by counsel.

Top three risks

  1. A demo statement mistaken for a compliance fact. Ticks beside encryption and access claims, a retention rule that does not run, and "Never uses account numbers" could be quoted by judges, a bank partner or users as real controls (SEC-01, SEC-02, SEC-03).
  2. Real statements imported into an unprotected browser store. The import path works with real bank CSVs today; their descriptions carry third party names and references and sit in plaintext localStorage on possibly shared phones, with no lock (SEC-03, SEC-04).
  3. Bolting on Supabase, an LLM or WhatsApp without the privacy plumbing. The README makes these swaps sound like one-file changes; each adds an operator, likely a cross-border transfer and new caching and logging paths (SEC-09, SEC-10, SEC-12).

Score

5 out of 10. Strong privacy UX for a demo and nothing leaves the device, but several screens assert controls that do not exist and none of the real-deployment foundations are in place.
