4.4Legal and regulatory advisor (South Africa)

Reviewer file: docs/review/legal.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

This is not legal advice. It is a simulated professional perspective for a hackathon review, based on public sources accessed on 2026-10-20 and the code at commit d114497; it does not replace an opinion from a South African attorney or a ruling from the FSCA, the NCR or the Information Regulator, and it does not claim knowledge of FNB's internal policy or licences. As a demo with fictional data and nothing sent, Tengo carries little legal risk today, and its "Simulated" labels are honest and well placed on the consent, Privacy and Inbox screens. As a real product the picture changes. Most of what Tengo says ("Enough until", "Safe to spend today", "Spend about R250 less a day") is cash flow guidance that does not relate to a financial product, so on the most likely reading it falls outside the FAIS definition of "advice" and needs no FSP licence. Three things break that comfort. First, "What helps most" can tell a user to pay a bill late, and the engine picks the bill by size alone, so it already tells Thabo to pay his car instalment after its due date (src/engine/help.ts:62, docs/review/screens/thabo-home-light-768.png). Pushing a credit instalment into arrears is the most harmful thing the app says, and the same logic would pick an insurance or funeral premium, which edges into FAIS advice on the "variation" or "termination" of a financial product. Second, the word "safe" is used as an unqualified promise ("Safe to spend", "Safe to lend up to") while the only "not financial advice" line sits at the bottom of a drawer most people will never open. Third, the daily WhatsApp and SMS messages default to on, carry no opt-out instruction or sender details, and would send a person's financial state through a foreign platform. None of this is fatal, and all of it is fixable in wording and engine rules before a pilot. The cheapest compliant route is to ship inside a licensed bank, under its FAIS licence, conduct standards and POPIA programme, rather than as a standalone fintech.

What works

  • Honest simulation labels. "Simulated privacy controls. Not a compliance certification." appears on the consent screen and the Privacy overview (src/pages/Consent.tsx:65, src/pages/privacy/Overview.tsx:56, docs/review/screens/thabo-consent-light-768.png). The Inbox says "Simulated. No message is sent." (src/pages/Inbox.tsx:55, docs/review/screens/thabo-inbox-dark-390.png). A judge or a user cannot reasonably mistake these for compliance claims.
  • Purpose limitation stated up front. The consent notice says data is used "Not for marketing or credit scoring" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:144). This aligns with POPIA section 18 notice duties and, if kept true, keeps Tengo away from POPIA section 71 automated decision rules and the section 69 direct marketing rules.
  • Granular consent with the right default for analytics. Forecast, messages and analytics are separate toggles; analytics is off by default (src/pages/Consent.tsx:27-28). Withdrawal pauses the forecast rather than silently continuing (src/i18n/locales/en.json:167, paused keys).
  • Explainability exists. The Why drawer shows the inputs, the method and the rules for Enough, Tight and Short in plain language (src/i18n/locales/en.json:403-439, docs/review/screens/thabo-why-drawer-dark-390.png). That is the kind of logic disclosure POPIA section 71(3) expects where automated decisions apply, and it supports CPA section 22 plain language.
  • Message content minimisation. Messages carry "Only your date, cover and safe to spend figure" and "Never your balance or transactions" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:657-658), which limits exposure on a lock screen.
  • Loan check is about the user's own money. It answers "what does lending to family do to my cover", it does not arrange, broker or price credit (src/engine/forecast.ts:346-367). Tengo is not a credit provider or intermediary on this design.

What to improve

LEGAL-01: "What helps most" tells users to pay credit instalments and insurance premiums late
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: src/engine/help.ts:55-71 picks the smallest bill before next money that, moved to after it, lifts the state; candidates are sorted by amount only (line 62) with no check of what the bill is. For Thabo it outputs "Pay Car instalment (R3 450) after Tue 27 Oct instead of before." (docs/review/prep/persona-snapshot.json, help.billToMove; docs/review/screens/thabo-home-light-768.png). Missing a vehicle finance instalment is a default under a credit agreement; it can lead to a section 129 notice, enforcement, repossession by court order and an adverse credit listing (cars.co.za and Moonstone summaries of NCA s129 and s130). Ayanda's recurring bills include a funeral policy and Thabo's include car insurance (persona-snapshot.json). Insurance policies are "financial products" under FAIS, and "advice" includes a recommendation "on the variation of any term or condition applying to a financial product ... or on the termination" of it (FAIS s1(1)(d)). A prompt to pay a premium late, which risks a lapse, is arguably such a recommendation. Tengo also cannot know whether the payee will accept a later date; most of these are debit orders.
  • Recommendation: (1) Classify bills into credit repayments, insurance and other, using description keywords plus a user confirmation step, and never suggest moving credit or insurance payments. (2) Reword the remaining suggestion to a question that keeps the decision with the user and states the risk: "Rent is due before your next money. If your landlord agrees to a later date, you would reach Tight. Ask first, and do not skip debit orders for loans or insurance." (3) Add a one-line inline note on the card: "Ideas, not advice. Check fees and penalties before changing a payment." (4) Add a unit test that a bill described as instalment, loan, finance, insurance, policy or premium is never returned as billToMove.
  • Effort: M
LEGAL-02: "Safe" is an unqualified promise and the disclaimer is hidden
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: "Safe to spend today", "Safe to lend up to", "Use R320" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:359,523; docs/review/screens/thabo-plan-dark-390.png). "Safe to lend" for Thabo keeps him only at Tight, meaning a heavy week does not last (src/i18n/locales/en.json:435,524). Ayanda is Short yet sees "Safe to spend today R310" (docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-light-390.png); the figure assumes income lands by the latest likely date (src/engine/forecast.ts:295-296). The only "not financial advice" text is why.notAdvice, rendered once at the foot of the Why drawer (src/components/forecast/WhyDrawer.tsx:145) and below the fold at 390px (docs/review/screens/thabo-why-drawer-dark-390.png). It is absent from Home, Plan, What helps most and every message. Where the CPA applies, a supplier must not use "exaggeration, innuendo or ambiguity" about a material fact (CPA s41(1)(b)) and notices must be understandable by an ordinary consumer with average literacy (s22(2)). A disclaimer also cannot turn advice into non-advice; the FAIS test looks at the substance of the recommendation (FAIS s1(1) and s1(3)(a); FIA commentary). Unclear law: whether the CPA covers a free app, or a service supplied by a bank that is regulated under financial sector laws, needs an attorney's view; treat CPA standards as the floor either way.
  • Recommendation: Rename to estimates: "Spend up to about R295 today" with sub-line "Based on past spending. Assumes your money lands by Tue 27 Oct." Rename "Safe to lend up to" to "You could lend up to" with "and stay at Tight. A heavy week would still run short." When Short, hide the spend figure or show "To last until your next money, keep to about R310 a day" in the Short colour. Put a short persistent line under the Home hero, the Plan cards and the Help card: "An estimate from your past patterns. Not financial advice." Keep the drawer text too.
  • Effort: S
LEGAL-03: Daily messages are opt-out by default and lack POPIA s69 elements
  • Severity: High (for any real send), Low (for the demo)
  • Evidence: The messages toggle starts on (src/pages/Consent.tsx:27, docs/review/screens/thabo-consent-light-768.png). POPIA defines consent as "any voluntary, specific and informed expression of will" (s1); a pre-set toggle bundled behind "Agree and continue" is weak evidence of that. Templates have no sender contact or opt-out route inside the message (src/i18n/locales/en.json:664-684, src/messages/templates.ts:21-35); "Turn off any time in Privacy" sits only in the app. The tips "A good week to put a little aside" are service guidance today, but the moment a bank partner adds a product nudge ("open a savings pocket") the message becomes direct marketing by electronic communication, which needs opt-in consent or the customer exception, plus sender identity and a cease address in every message (POPIA s69(1), (3), (4)). The Information Regulator's December 2024 Guidance Note on direct marketing says opting out is not consent. Meta requires opt-in that names the business before business-initiated WhatsApp messages, using approved templates. WASPA's Code has a "Reply STOP" requirement for SMS marketing.
  • Recommendation: Default messages to off and ask on the Inbox with a separate, named opt-in: "Send me a daily Tengo update on WhatsApp at 07:00. I can reply STOP at any time." Record channel, phone, wording version and timestamp in the consent log. Add "Reply STOP to end. Tengo, [contact]" to every SMS and WhatsApp template (SMS budget: the current SMS fits in one segment, so check the 160 character limit in templates.test.ts). Keep service messages free of any product promotion; if a partner wants promotional content, collect a separate marketing consent.
  • Effort: S
LEGAL-04: No responsible party, terms of use or complaints route for a real launch
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: The Information Officer is a placeholder (src/i18n/locales/en.json:239-244); there is no operator name, address, terms of use or complaints process anywhere in the locale file (grep for "terms" returns nothing). POPIA s18(1) requires the name and address of the responsible party, the purpose, whether supply is voluntary, cross-border plans and the right to complain. ECTA s43(1) requires suppliers transacting electronically to show full name, legal status, physical address, contact details and privacy policy on the site. Unclear law: ECTA s43 is framed around goods or services offered by electronic transaction; a free app may fall outside, but a paid tier or a bank-embedded service likely does not.
  • Recommendation: Before any pilot, publish: operator legal name, registration number and address; terms of use (estimate only, no guarantee, no liability for decisions taken, how to complain); a POPIA privacy notice; and the Information Officer registered with the Information Regulator. If embedded in a bank, use the bank's existing notices and complaints process instead.
  • Effort: M
LEGAL-05: Regulatory perimeter not documented; FAIS position depends on product links
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: FAIS "advice" means a recommendation, guidance or proposal of a financial nature "in respect of" the purchase of, investment in, transactions on, or variation or termination of a financial product (FAIS s1(1)). Financial products include insurance policies and deposits as defined in the Banks Act (FAIS s1(1), paragraph (f) of "financial product"). Daily spend figures and "spend less" suggestions do not name a product, so the likely interpretation is that they are not advice. The alternative interpretation is that guidance on drawing down a bank deposit, or on setting money aside ("You could set aside up to R280", src/i18n/locales/en.json:702), is guidance in respect of a deposit; FAIS s1(4) limits FAIS for short-term bank deposits to what the code of conduct regulates, which reduces but does not remove this risk. Once a bank links Tengo's "set aside" to a named savings product, it is automated advice and falls under the bank's FAIS licence and the FSCA's fit and proper rules for automated advice (key individual who understands the algorithm, ongoing monitoring; Profile summary of FSCA requirements). The COFI Bill was introduced to the National Assembly on 17 April 2026 (Clyde & Co); I could not source its final advice definitions, so treat activity-based conduct regulation and "fair customer outcomes" as the direction of travel, not settled law.
  • Recommendation: Write a one-page perimeter memo, reviewed by an attorney, covering FAIS, NCA, CPA and POPIA, with a rule: Tengo never names, compares or links to a financial product unless operated inside a licensed FSP. Keep "set aside" generic. If a bank pilots it, the bank's compliance function should classify it as either factual information (FAIS s1(3)(a)) or automated advice, and test it against the bank's Conduct Standard obligations (FSCA Conduct Standard 3 of 2020 requires banks to prioritise fair treatment of financial customers).
  • Effort: S (memo), L (bank onboarding)
LEGAL-06: "Bring in R380 more" can steer people towards expensive or informal credit
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: help.extra: "Bring in {{amount}} more before {{date}}." (src/i18n/locales/en.json:699; src/components/forecast/HelpCard.tsx:45). For a Short user the fastest way to "bring in" money is often a loan. Tengo would not be giving credit advice in the regulated sense (there is no NCA licence category for general money tips, and debt counselling is the specific section 86 process run by a registered natural person), but the wording invites borrowing without a warning.
  • Recommendation: Reword to "Earn or receive R380 more before Sun 25 Oct, for example an extra shift or money owed to you. Borrowing to cover a gap usually costs more later." Add a link to the NCR's consumer information and, for Short users, a neutral line pointing to free help such as the NCR's debt counselling information, without recommending any provider.
  • Effort: S
LEGAL-07: Cross-border transfer and sensitive inference in WhatsApp messages
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: WhatsApp is operated by Meta outside South Africa. A message saying "Your money may run out on Fri 23 Oct ... Cover: Short" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:668-669) discloses financial distress to a foreign processor and to anyone who sees a shared or locked phone. POPIA s72(1) permits transfer abroad only with adequate protection, consent, contractual necessity or similar grounds. The Privacy centre's cross-border card is simulated.
  • Recommendation: Name WhatsApp and Meta in the message opt-in and the privacy notice as a cross-border recipient, rely on explicit consent under s72(1)(b), offer SMS as the alternative, and offer a "discreet" template that omits the state word ("Tengo: your update is ready. Open the app.").
  • Effort: S
LEGAL-08: State label contradicts itself on Home
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Thabo's hero reads "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" next to a "Tight" chip, and the 30 day chart header shows "Enough" (docs/review/screens/thabo-home-light-768.png; src/i18n/locales/en.json:346). "Enough" is used both as a state and as a date label. Under CPA s22(2) an ordinary consumer should understand the notice "without undue effort"; mixed signals about whether one is covered are a misrepresentation risk under s41(1)(b).
  • Recommendation: Use "Lasts until Tue 27 Oct" for the hero in all non-Short states, and reserve "Enough" for the state chip only.
  • Effort: S
LEGAL-09: Future bank use could trigger POPIA s71 and purpose creep
  • Severity: Low (today), Medium (in a bank)
  • Evidence: POPIA s71 bars decisions with legal consequences based solely on automated profiling of, among other things, creditworthiness and reliability. Tengo's states are not used for any decision today, and the notice says "Not for marketing or credit scoring" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:144). A bank partner may want to reuse Enough, Tight and Short for limits, overdraft offers or collections timing.
  • Recommendation: Put the purpose limit into any partner contract: Tengo outputs may not be used for credit decisions, pricing, collections or marketing without a fresh, separate consent and a s71 assessment. Keep the "Question this result" route and make it reach a human in production.
  • Effort: S

Top three risks

  1. Harm from late payment suggestions. A user follows "Pay Car instalment after Tue 27 Oct" and falls into arrears, fees or repossession steps, or lets a funeral or car insurance policy lapse. This is the most likely route to a real complaint, a reputational incident for a bank partner, and an argument that Tengo gave FAIS advice on an insurance product (LEGAL-01).
  2. "Safe" read as a guarantee. A Tight or Short user spends or lends the "safe" amount, income arrives late, and the user blames the app. The hidden disclaimer will not help, and CPA s41 standards on ambiguity apply where the CPA applies (LEGAL-02, LEGAL-08).
  3. Messaging consent failure at scale. Default-on daily messages without in-message opt-out, and any later product nudges, breach POPIA s69 and Meta's opt-in rules, risking Information Regulator enforcement and WhatsApp account suspension (LEGAL-03, LEGAL-07).

Score

5/10: honest demo labels and a sensible privacy posture, but the engine can recommend paying credit and insurance late, "safe" wording overpromises, and the messaging consent design would not survive a real launch.

4.5Credit and finance risk analyst

Reviewer file: docs/review/credit-risk.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

The engine is small, pure and explainable, and its core choice (count only money already in the account, run it down at a rolling 7 day rate) is sensibly conservative for the headline date. The risk sits elsewhere. The cover state, "Safe to spend" and "Safe to lend up to" all assume the next money will arrive, and nothing checks whether it still does: income that stopped five weeks ago, a three week holiday, or an overdue lump all still produce Enough, high confidence and a lending figure of most of the balance. Every "safe" figure is sized to leave about R0 on payday, and the state looks no further than payday, so a rent payment due the day after shows as Enough with R1 090 "safe to lend". Zero income can show Enough. One-off spends, windfalls and a changed bill amount distort the rates or drop bills entirely. For a product aimed at people whose income is irregular by definition, these are the failure modes that matter most, and the unit tests do not cover any of them. Fixable in days, but not safe for real users as it stands.

What works

  • Conservative headline. The runway counts only money already in the account and ignores expected income, so the date errs early. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts:196 to 211, README "How the forecast works" step 4.
  • Rolling 7 day spend instead of single-day medians. Copes with lumpy stock runs and weekly grocery shops; heavy and light paths use the same basis. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts:59 to 88, README "Decisions" (Spend paths).
  • Safe to spend uses the latest likely next money date, not the typical one, and nets off bills due before it. Following Ayanda's R310 every day leaves R17 on 26 Oct rather than running out. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts:291 to 296; docs/review/tools/credit-risk/results.md row P-ayanda.
  • Overdrawn and Short cases fail safe on lending. A balance of minus R800 gives Short, R0 safe to spend and R0 safe to lend. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts:202, 358; results row S03.
  • Overdue interval income never lands in the past. The overdue rule moves the window to start tomorrow. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts:178 to 182.
  • Everything is reproducible and inspectable. One engine powers date, what-if, loan check and help, and the "Why this date" drawer shows the inputs. Engine statement coverage is 97.6 percent. Evidence: src/engine/help.ts:24 to 26, docs/review/prep/coverage.txt, fact sheet.

What to improve

Stress test: docs/review/tools/credit-risk/stress.ts (run with npx vite-node docs/review/tools/credit-risk/stress.ts), results in docs/review/tools/credit-risk/results.md and results.csv. Scenario ids below (S01 to S13, P-persona) refer to those rows. Constructed histories are synthetic: weekly R2 000 pay on Fridays, R60 to R160 daily spend, rent on the 1st unless stated.

RISK-01: Income that has stopped is still treated as coming

Severity: High

Evidence: The weekday rule only needs 60 percent of qualifying deposits on one weekday and has no recency check; the interval rule, when income is overdue, simply restarts the window from tomorrow (src/engine/forecast.ts:144 to 166, 178 to 182). Confidence depends only on history length (forecast.ts:30 to 34). Results: - S11, weekly pay stopped 35 days ago (five missed paydays): next money "23 Oct, R2 000", state Enough, confidence high, safe to lend R1 990 of a R2 500 balance. - S09, salon closed for 21 days with no income: Enough, next money Friday, safe to lend R990. - S11b, lumpy deposits, none for 40 days: next money 21 to 23 Oct, Enough, safe to lend R2 110. No unit test covers stopped or paused income (src/engine/forecast.test.ts test list).

Recommendation: Count expected paydays that did not happen since the last qualifying deposit. Weekday pattern: if the last qualifying deposit is more than 10 days old, treat the pattern as broken. Interval pattern: if today is past last deposit plus the 80th percentile gap plus one typical gap, treat it as broken. When broken: pattern becomes "paused", the state can be at most Tight, safe to lend is R0, confidence drops to low, and the copy says "Your usual money has not come in since 14 Sep. Tengo is not counting on it until it does." Add unit tests for S09, S11 and S11b.

Effort: S

RISK-02: Every "safe" figure leaves nothing on payday, and the state ignores what is due just after payday

Severity: High

Evidence: Safe to spend spreads the balance to exactly zero at the latest next money (forecast.ts:294 to 296): following it leaves R32 (Thabo), R18 (Lerato) and R17 (Ayanda) (rows P-thabo, P-lerato, P-ayanda). Safe to lend is the largest amount that keeps the state (forecast.ts:357 to 367); in every Enough scenario the heavy-week path is left at R0 to R10 on the latest payday (column "heavy left after lending", rows S01, S02b, S05, S09, S11, S12b, S13). The state checks only that the money reaches next money (forecast.ts:217 to 221), never whether next money covers what follows. S13: rent R3 000 due 25 Oct, the day after the latest next money, weekly pay about R2 000. Engine: Enough, safe to spend R400 a day, safe to lend R1 090. Following either leaves the user unable to pay rent.

Recommendation: (1) Extend the cover check one cycle: Enough also requires balance minus spend to next money, plus the low next-money amount, to cover heavy spend and bills up to the payday after. (2) Keep a buffer in both "safe" figures. Assumption: one heavy day of spend or 10 percent of the balance, whichever is larger, is a reasonable starting buffer (plausible range: one heavy day to one heavy week); validate with the backtest. (3) Subtract bills due within 7 days after next money that the low next-money amount cannot cover. (4) Cap safe to lend at the smaller of the state-keeping amount and the balance left after a full heavy week.

Effort: M

RISK-03: No income can show Enough, and Short still talks about "your next money"

Severity: High

Evidence: With no qualifying deposits the engine invents a next money 30 days out (forecast.ts:107 to 119, UNKNOWN_INCOME_DAYS at forecast.ts:28) and then grades cover against it. S02b, 90 days with no income and R9 000 in the account: Enough, safe to lend R2 670. S02, R4 000: Short, and the state card says "Runs out N days before your next money" (src/i18n/locales/en.json stateCard.short_other) although there is no next money. S07-5 and S07-7 (5 and 7 days of history, one payday seen) fall into the same 30 day plan. A unit test asserts Enough with no history at all (src/engine/forecast.test.ts:116 to 122). A windfall can also trigger this path (RISK-05).

Recommendation: When the pattern is unknown, do not show Enough, Tight or Short. Show "No regular income found" as the state, keep the date as "Money lasts until 2 Dec if nothing comes in", set safe to lend to R0, and let the user enter an expected date and amount. Change the no-history test to expect no state.

Effort: S

RISK-04: "Safe to spend" contradicts the state and is not a safe amount

Severity: High

Evidence: Ayanda is Short from 23 Oct, yet "Safe to spend today R310" (docs/review/prep/persona-snapshot.json, walkthrough item 2, docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-dark-1280.png). The two numbers use different rules: the state uses the usual spend rate against the typical next money, the figure divides the balance by days to the latest next money (forecast.ts:217 to 221 versus 291 to 296). R310 is 50 percent of her usual R623 a day, and about 88 percent of her everyday spend is wholesale stock (R15 137 of the 30 day breakdown), so halving it may cut the income that pays for next week. For Lerato the same label shows R330, 274 percent of her usual R120 (row P-lerato), which reads as permission to spend nearly three times her norm. The SMS repeats it: "money may run out {{date}} (Short). Safe today {{sts}}" (en.json messages.sms.short). The "not financial advice" line appears only inside the Why drawer (src/components/forecast/WhyDrawer.tsx:145).

Recommendation: Rename and reword by state. Short: "To reach 26 Oct, keep to R310 a day. You usually spend R620." Tight: "Daily limit to reach 27 Oct: R295." Enough: "You can spend up to R330 a day until 25 Oct. You usually spend R120." Never use the word "safe" when the state is Short. Show the usual daily spend beside the figure. Drop "Safe today" from the Short SMS and say "Check Tengo before spending today." Put the estimate line under the figure on Home, not only in the drawer.

Effort: S

RISK-05: One-off spends, windfalls and loans distort rates, income detection and help

Severity: High

Evidence: No outlier handling in spend rates (forecast.ts:66 to 82) or income totals (forecast.ts:128 to 131). S08, two R9 000 one-offs (car repair, funeral) in 60 days: heavy rate R1 393 a day against R127 normally, state Tight, help says "cut R645 a day". S08b, one R6 000 repair in 20 days of history: band widens to 25 days. S04, a R40 000 stokvel payout lifts "typical monthly income" so the 10 percent threshold (R2 200) exceeds the R2 000 weekly pay: the pattern becomes unknown and a person with steady pay shows Short with "next money" a month away. S04b: a R15 000 loan disbursement counts as income because every positive amount is income (src/engine/classify.ts:66).

Recommendation: Winsorise daily spend before the rolling sums (cap each day at, for example, the 95th percentile of daily spend, and list capped items as "one-offs" the user can confirm). Base the significance threshold on median monthly income or exclude the single largest deposit. Tag transfers, reversals and loan payouts (description keywords plus a user toggle) as "not income". Add unit tests for S04, S04b and S08.

Effort: M

RISK-06: Bill detection breaks on a price rise or a skipped month, and arrears are invisible

Severity: Medium

Evidence: All amounts must be within 20 percent of the median and every gap 24 to 36 days (classify.ts:16 to 22, 44 to 54). S10, rent R2 000, R2 000, R2 600: all three become everyday spend, no November rent is projected, the heavy rate triples to R379 and the state is still Enough. S10b, a 15 percent rise: projected rent stays R2 000, the median of the last three (classify.ts:88). S05b, a skipped September: rent becomes spend. S05, October rent unpaid: the engine projects the next rent on 1 Nov and treats the missed R2 500 as never owed; state Enough, safe to lend R690. Only monthly bills exist, so weekly stokvel contributions or termly school fees are everyday spend.

Recommendation: Allow one missing cycle (gap up to 2 times the cycle) and judge tolerance against the latest amount, projecting the latest amount when the last two agree. When an expected bill has not appeared 5 days after its usual date, ask "Did you pay Rent this month?" and, if not, add it as due now. Let users mark and edit bills. Support weekly and fortnightly cycles.

Effort: M

RISK-07: Month-end and seasonal spikes are averaged away

Severity: Medium

Evidence: The median of rolling 7 day sums ignores calendar position. S06, R600 a day extra spending from the 25th, today 24 Oct, balance R3 000: usual rate R128, headline "Enough until 16 Nov", safe to spend R425. The known spike costs about R4 260 over 25 to 30 Oct, so the balance is gone around 28 Oct. The README admits no month-end or seasonality modelling (README.md:319). Irregular earners in South Africa often face month-end and December pressure (Assumption, based on salaried customers and school fees spending around paydays and January; not sourced).

Recommendation: With 60 or more days of history, build a day-of-month uplift (ratio of spend in days 25 to 31 versus the rest) and apply it to the projected days that fall in that window. At minimum, flag "Month end is usually heavier for you" when the ratio exceeds 1.5.

Effort: M

RISK-08: Confidence and the range band do not reflect real uncertainty

Severity: Medium

Evidence: Confidence is history length only (forecast.ts:30 to 34). The band is the spread of rolling weekly spend, so with few windows it collapses: S07-5 and S07-7 show a 0 day band at low confidence (one rolling window, so all percentiles are equal and 1.5 times zero is zero); S07-14 shows a 0 day band, an interval pattern inferred from just two paydays, Enough and safe to lend R1 150. S11 shows high confidence after income stopped. The band ignores uncertainty in the income date and amount.

Recommendation: Score confidence on history length, number of qualifying deposits (at least 4), gap regularity, recency of last income and outlier share. Enforce a minimum band (Assumption: plus or minus 1 day per week of runway at low confidence, range 0.5 to 2 days). Hide safe to lend at low confidence and say why.

Effort: S

RISK-09: "What helps most" can recommend harmful actions

Severity: Medium

Evidence: The bill to move is any bill before next money whose deferral reaches the target (src/engine/help.ts:58 to 74). It names Thabo's R3 450 car instalment (P-thabo, walkthrough item 10) and, with zero income, suggests moving R2 500 rent (S02), where deferral may mean penalties, arrears or eviction risk. The daily cut is the money gap divided by days (help.ts:56), so for Ayanda it is R250 a day out of R623, mostly stock, and after a one-off repair it is R645 a day (S08). Copy: "Pay {{name}} ({{amount}}) after {{date}} instead of before." (en.json help.moveBill).

Recommendation: Never suggest moving rent, credit instalments, insurance or funeral policies unless the user marks the bill as flexible; when shown, say "Ask {{name}} first if you can pay on {{date}}. Late payments can cost fees or hurt your credit record." Separate business stock from household spend (the spending groups already find "stock") and phrase cuts on household spend only: "Spend about R55 less a day on household costs until 27 Oct." Cap the suggested cut at a share of usual spend (Assumption: 25 percent, range 15 to 35) and fall back to "Bring in R1 240 more" beyond that.

Effort: S

RISK-10: Payday morning shows Short

Severity: Medium

Evidence: Next money is "always after today" (forecast.ts:103, 106, 149), so if the usual payday is today and the payout has not cleared, it is pushed a full week. S12, Tuesday payout due today, R400 in the account: Short, next money 27 Oct, help says "bring in R420". Once paid (S12b) the same person is Enough. This invites alarm and panic borrowing on the morning pay is due.

Recommendation: If today is the expected payday and no qualifying deposit has landed today, set earliest and typical to today and latest to tomorrow, and show "Payday today, not in yet" in place of Short until the latest date passes.

Effort: S

RISK-11: Safe to lend wording implies safety and ignores repayment

Severity: Medium

Evidence: "Safe to lend up to" and "Lending up to this keeps you at {{state}}" (en.json plan.safeToLend, plan.safeToLendHint, shown at src/pages/Plan.tsx:204 to 206), with a one-tap "Use R…" button (Plan.tsx:236 to 238). The figure assumes the money never returns but does not say so, and in Tight it keeps only the normal-week path alive, so lending Thabo's R320 leaves his heavy week R695 short (row P-thabo).

Recommendation: Rename to "You could lend up to R320". Hint: "You would still reach payday on a normal week. Only lend what you can manage without getting it back." In Tight add "A heavy week would leave you short." Remove the one-tap fill or show it only in Enough.

Effort: S

RISK-12: Small daily income is ignored in the headline, so states skew low and conflict with the chart

Severity: Low

Evidence: Deposits under 10 percent of monthly income are background income used only in the scrubber (forecast.ts:135, 259 to 262). Ignored per day: Thabo R107, Lerato R170, Ayanda R57 (persona rows). This is part of why Ayanda's headline says Short while the chart for today says Enough (walkthrough item 3).

Recommendation: Add half the background rate to the central path (the heavy path keeps none), or at least show "Not counting about R107 a day in small takings" in the Why drawer. Keep the headline conservative but explain it.

Effort: S

Proposed wording, summary
Where Now Proposed
Home figure, Short Safe to spend today R310 To reach 26 Oct, keep to R310 a day. You usually spend R620.
Home figure, Enough Safe to spend today R330 You can spend up to R330 a day until 25 Oct. You usually spend R120.
State, no income Short / Runs out N days before your next money No regular income found. Money lasts until 2 Dec if nothing comes in.
State, income paused Enough Your usual money has not come in since 14 Sep. Tengo is not counting on it.
Payday today Short Payday today, not in yet.
Plan Safe to lend up to R1 090 You could lend up to R1 090. Only lend what you can manage without getting it back.
Help, bill Pay Car instalment (R3 450) after 27 Oct instead of before. Ask the lender first if you can pay on 28 Oct. Late payments can cost fees or hurt your credit record.
SMS, Short Safe today R310. Check Tengo before spending today.
Under every figure (drawer only) An estimate from your past money, not advice.

Top three risks

  1. False Enough when income has stopped or never existed (RISK-01, RISK-03): a person who lost platform access or closed for the holidays is told they are covered and can lend most of their balance.
  2. Zero-buffer "safe" figures that look only to payday (RISK-02, RISK-04, RISK-11): following the figures exactly leaves R0 to R30 on payday and can leave rent unpaid the day after, while the word "safe" carries the authority of a bank.
  3. Harmful nudges from distorted inputs (RISK-05, RISK-09): one-off costs and windfalls swing the state, and the help card can tell someone to defer rent or a car instalment or to halve stock purchases that generate their income.

Score

4/10: clean, explainable and conservative on the date, but the state and "safe" figures fail exactly the irregular-income cases the product is built for, and none of them is tested.

Sources

None. All evidence is from the repository and the stress test in docs/review/tools/credit-risk/. Items marked Assumption are unsourced judgements with stated ranges.

4.6Data scientist (forecast validation)

Reviewer file: docs/review/data-scientist.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

What this backtest can and cannot show. All data here is synthetic. The seed generator (src/data/seed/generate.ts) and the engine (src/engine/forecast.ts) were written by the same author with matching assumptions: income on a fixed weekday or at bounded intervals, bills on a fixed day of the month for a fixed amount, everyday spend drawn from the same distribution every day, no missed payouts, no shocks, no cash the bank cannot see. The engine was in effect designed for this world, so good numbers here are weak evidence of real-world accuracy. They show the engine is internally consistent and they locate structural weaknesses (which are real, because they appear even in this friendly world). They do not show that a real irregular earner would see a date within two days of reality. The shipped generator is also thinner than it looks: across 24 cut-off dates it produces only 7 distinct random paths for Thabo, 7 for Lerato and 1 for Ayanda (results/generator_diversity.csv). Real-data validation is described at the end of this file.

Verdict

On data from its own generator the engine is reasonably accurate on the headline date (mean absolute error 2.1 days with 90 days of history, bias near zero, beats a naive balance divided by average spend baseline at 3.4 days) and its range band covers the actual date 72 percent of the time overall. That headline hides three structural problems that appear even in this friendly setting: the band is too narrow exactly when the user is close to running out (54 percent coverage when money runs out within 6 days, with 28 percent running out before the "heavy week" edge); the Tight state is followed by running short 24 to 35 percent of the time and Enough is followed by running short 12 percent of the time for the lumpy spaza owner; and with 14 to 28 days of history, which the CSV import accepts, monthly bills are invisible and the engine is worse than the naive baseline and biased optimistic by up to 5.5 days. None of this has been tested on real statements, so every number below is an upper bound on what to expect in the field.

What works

  • Headline date is accurate on its own data. Seeds, 90 day history, 2 157 observed cases: mean absolute error 2.1 days (95 percent bootstrap interval 1.95 to 2.20), mean signed error 0.0 days, 78 percent within 3 days. Source: docs/review/backtest/results/summary_headline.csv, results/bootstrap_ci.csv, chart docs/review/backtest/charts/error_distribution.png.
  • It beats a naive baseline once it has 60 or more days. Naive balance divided by 28 day mean outflow: 3.4 days MAE against the engine's 2.1 (90 days) and 2.2 (60 days). The recurring bill detection (src/engine/classify.ts:16) is what earns this. Source: results/summary_by_history.csv.
  • Overall band coverage is near its implied level and misses are balanced. The band is the 20th to 80th percentile of weekly spend (src/engine/forecast.ts:81-82), which implies roughly 60 percent coverage. Observed: 71.8 percent (interval 69.1 to 74.4), 13.5 percent before the band and 14.7 percent after. Source: results/summary_headline.csv, charts/calibration.png.
  • Enough is reliable for regular earners. For Lerato no Enough or Tight case ran short (0 of 595), for Thabo 6 of 450 Enough cases ran short (1.3 percent). Source: results/cases.csv via the confusion breakdown in this file.
  • Next money is right for weekday earners. Thabo and Lerato: next money date exact in 100 percent of cases with 28 or more days of history. Source: results/summary_by_persona_history_seeds.csv.
  • The engine is pure, deterministic and fast enough to backtest at scale. 12 528 forecasts (plus bisection inside startingBalance) ran in about 55 seconds with no mocks. This makes continuous accuracy testing cheap. Source: docs/review/backtest/backtest.ts, results/console.txt.

Method

Data
  • Personas: thabo (weekly app payout plus daily cash fares), lerato (Saturday takings), ayanda (lumpy cash deposits every 4 to 9 days, irregular stock runs).
  • Cut-offs: 24 per persona, every 15 days from 2025-10-06 to 2026-09-11, so they cover 12 months, every weekday and month boundaries.
  • Source "shipped": generateHistory(persona, cutoff + 30) returns 90 days ending cutoff + 29. The first 60 days are history, the last 30 the hold-out.
  • Source "seeds": a copy of the persona rules from generate.ts with the seed and length as parameters. The script asserts it reproduces generateHistory exactly for the shipped seed on three dates before running. Seeds tengo:<persona>:backtest<k>:<cutoff>, k = 1 to 5, 120 days: 90 history, 30 hold-out. This gives 120 independent paths per persona. I added it because the shipped generator repeats itself (see the box above).
  • History length: truncated to the last 14, 28, 60 (both sources) and 90 days (seeds) before the cut-off, then reclassified with classifyRows on the history only, so future bill occurrences cannot leak into classification.
  • Balance at the cut-off (known): six per case. Three from startingBalance with targets enough, tight and short (this uses the engine itself, so it is circular and reported separately). Three engine-independent: 7, 14 and 21 days of the history's mean daily outflow.
  • Case counts: 9 936 unperturbed cases (1 296 shipped, 8 640 seeds) and 2 592 perturbed cases.
Measures (day index 0 is the cut-off, the engine's "today")
  • Actual runway date (engine definition): first day on which balance minus cumulative actual outflows (everyday spend and bills, from day 0) is zero or below, ignoring all new income. This matches zeroDate (src/engine/forecast.ts:196). Censored if not reached within 30 days (0.1 percent of seeds cases).
  • Runway error: engine central date minus actual runway date, in days. Positive means the forecast was too late, that is too optimistic. MAE is over observed cases.
  • Actual run-out including real income: first day the real balance (including all real income from day 1) is zero or below. Day 0 income is treated as already in the balance, as the engine does (src/engine/forecast.ts:124).
  • Band coverage: share of decidable cases where the actual runway date is inside [heavy-path date, light-path date] inclusive (src/engine/forecast.ts:305). Undecidable when the actual is censored and the band reaches past day 29. Misses are split into "early" (ran out before the heavy-path edge, the dangerous direction) and "late".
  • Next money accuracy: actual next money is the first day from day 1 whose income is at least the engine's own significance threshold (10 percent of typical monthly income, computed from the history). Reported as MAE of the typical date, percent exact, and percent inside the engine's earliest to latest window.
  • State in hindsight: let A be the actual next money day and L the balance after outflows on days 0 to A-1 (no income). Short if L is zero or below (ran out before next money, the engine's convention that running out on the arrival day counts as covered). Enough if L is at least 20 percent of the outflows of that period (it would have survived a heavier stretch). Tight otherwise. Sensitivity with 10 and 30 percent is in the CSVs and changes overall accuracy by under 1 point for seeds.
  • Covered accuracy (binary): engine Enough or Tight against hindsight not Short. False reassurance: share of all cases where the engine showed Enough or Tight and the hindsight state was Short.
  • Real shortfall: the real balance with all real income hit zero before the next money.
  • Baseline: balance divided by the mean daily outflow (spend and bills) of the last 28 days of history.
  • Uncertainty: 95 percent bootstrap intervals, resampling the 360 independent seed paths with the six balances of a path kept together (bootstrap.py, results/bootstrap_ci.csv). Shipped-source numbers have no meaningful interval because they rest on 15 distinct paths.

Backtest summary

Primary row for citing: seeds, 90 day history, all personas. "Cov" is band coverage; "early" is the share that ran out before the heavy-week edge. State accuracy is 3-class with the 20 percent buffer rule.
