Other results
  • By lead time (seeds, 90 day; results/summary_by_lead_time.csv): when money actually ran out on day 0 to 6, band coverage 53.5 percent with 27.5 percent before the heavy edge, state accuracy 49.8 percent, false reassurance 19.0 percent. Day 7 to 13: coverage 80.4 percent. Day 14 to 20: 88.5 percent. Day 21 to 29: 87.1 percent.
  • Shown state against hindsight (seeds, 90 day; charts/state_confusion.png): Enough shown 1 349 times, 93 percent Enough, 2 percent Tight, 4.3 percent Short. Tight shown 434 times, 66 percent Enough, 9 percent Tight, 24.4 percent Short. Short shown 377 times, 67 percent Short, 33 percent covered. By persona, P(Short in hindsight given shown): Thabo Enough 1.3, Tight 32.9; Lerato Enough 0.0, Tight 0.0; Ayanda Enough 11.6, Tight 39.7. Shown Short was right 88.5 percent for Thabo, 60.0 for Lerato, 51.6 for Ayanda.
  • Real money, with real income: the engine's runway date fell inside the 30 day hold-out in 99.9 percent of seeds cases, but the real balance reached zero within 30 days in only 20.8 percent. When the engine showed Short, the real balance hit zero before the next money in 53.1 percent of cases (background income such as card sales and cash fares carried the rest).
  • At the persona's own demo balance level (startingBalance target enough, seeds 90): Ayanda ran short in hindsight in 29 of 120 Enough cases (24 percent); Thabo 6 of 120; Lerato 0.
  • Perturbations (shipped, 60 day, 432 cases each; results/perturbation.csv, charts/perturbation.png): MAE / band coverage / false reassurance went from 1.9 / 65.7 / 7.6 with no change to: noisier daily spend 2.2 / 63.1 / 8.6; new R800 debit order 2.8 / 56.7 / 18.5; prices up 25 percent 2.9 / 52.8 / 19.9; one R2 000 emergency 3.4 / 47.5 / 17.4; income 3 days late 1.9 / 65.7 / 15.0 (next money in window falls from 100 to 0 percent); one payout missed 1.9 / 65.7 / 36.8.

What to improve

DATA-01 Validation is circular and the evidence base is thin
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: The generator encodes the engine's assumptions: monthly bills on a fixed day at a fixed amount (src/data/seed/generate.ts:18-20), income on a fixed weekday or a bounded interval (generate.ts:26, 40, 72-76), no missed payouts, no shocks. startingBalance picks balances with the engine itself (generate.ts:117-149). The shipped seed is fixed per persona (generate.ts:97) and the random stream only depends on weekday alignment, so 24 cut-offs give 7, 7 and 1 distinct paths (results/generator_diversity.csv); Ayanda's demo history is the same on every date. The engine unit tests check rules, not accuracy (src/engine/forecast.test.ts). There is no real-data result anywhere in the repository.
  • Recommendation: Treat every number in this file as an upper bound. Do not quote "2 day accuracy" in the pitch without "on synthetic data". Run the real-data validation below before any pilot claim. Make the generator seed vary with the date so demo data is not one path.
  • Effort: L
DATA-02 Tight and Enough give false reassurance for lumpy earners
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Seeds, 90 day: when Tight is shown ("Covered on a normal week, not on a heavy one", src/i18n/locales/en.json:366) the user ran out before the next money in 24.4 percent of cases (Ayanda 39.7, Thabo 32.9). Enough ("Covered until your next money, even on a heavy week", en.json:365) was followed by running short 11.6 percent of the time for Ayanda and 24 percent at Ayanda's own demo balance level. Overall false reassurance 7.6 percent (6.1 to 9.2). The cause: Enough only stresses spend with a constant 80th percentile weekly rate (forecast.ts:81) and income by one day for weekday patterns (forecast.ts:156); large irregular outflows such as stock runs are smoothed into the rate.
  • Recommendation: Set state thresholds from a target error rate, for example "Enough means under 3 percent chance of running short", estimated by simulation over resampled historical weeks and income gaps. Model large irregular outflows as discrete events. Make the Tight copy say plainly that running short is a real possibility. Report P(short) per state as a tracked metric.
  • Effort: M
DATA-03 The band is too narrow when it matters most and too wide when it does not
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Seeds, 90 day: when money ran out within 6 days, only 53.5 percent of actual dates fell in the band and 27.5 percent fell before the heavy-week edge; at 14 to 29 days coverage is 87 to 88 percent (results/summary_by_lead_time.csv, charts/calibration.png). The band applies one constant percentile rate over the whole horizon (forecast.ts:81-82, 196-213), which ignores that a single fuel fill or stock run dominates a short horizon while weekly variation averages out over a long one. Ayanda coverage is 59.0 percent with 23.3 percent early misses. The band is labelled "Likely between" (en.json:353) with no stated coverage.
  • Recommendation: Build the band from simulated paths (block bootstrap of historical days or weeks) and take date quantiles, so width grows with horizon correctly and lumpy outflows are represented. Pick and state a nominal coverage (for example 80 percent) and track empirical coverage by lead time.
  • Effort: M
DATA-04 Short histories hide bills and the engine becomes worse than a naive baseline
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Import accepts 14 days and only warns under 28 (src/lib/csv.ts:6-7). A bill needs 2 occurrences 24 to 36 days apart (src/engine/classify.ts:16-21), so no monthly bill can be detected under about 25 days and few under 60. Seeds: MAE 5.4 days at 14 days against a baseline of 4.2, and 3.9 at 28 days against 3.3. Lerato (rent R2 800 on the 1st) is biased optimistic by +5.5 days at 14 days and +3.6 days at 28. The low-confidence widening (forecast.ts:84-85) does not fix it: band coverage at 14 days is 59.2 percent.
  • Recommendation: Treat a single large debit as a provisional monthly bill (or ask the user to confirm "Is this rent? Monthly?"), or fall back to mean total outflow when history is under 60 days. Do not show Enough with under 28 days of history.
  • Effort: M
DATA-05 Errors grow quickly under shocks the generator never produces
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: One payout missed raises false reassurance from 7.6 to 36.8 percent; prices up 25 percent to 19.9; a new R800 debit order to 18.5; one R2 000 emergency to 17.4 with band coverage down to 47.5 percent (results/perturbation.csv). These events are ordinary for informal earners. The engine has no notion of income risk beyond a one day delay for weekday patterns (forecast.ts:156).
  • Recommendation: Add an explicit "what if the next payout is missed" check to the state rule for Enough, update rates faster after a regime change (weight recent weeks more), and surface new debit orders as soon as they appear once.
  • Effort: M
DATA-06 Next money is weak for interval income
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: Ayanda (interval pattern, seeds 90): next money inside the earliest to latest window in 57.5 percent of cases, exact 16.7 percent, MAE 2.1 days. Weekday personas score 100 percent only because the generator never misses or shifts a payout; with income 3 days late, window accuracy falls to 0 percent (results/perturbation.csv). The weekday window is hard-coded to typical plus one day (forecast.ts:156).
  • Recommendation: Estimate the weekday window from observed lateness in the history, widen the interval window (20th to 80th percentile of 8 to 13 gaps is noisy), and show next money as a range in the copy.
  • Effort: S
DATA-07 The headline date is a counterfactual that never happens for most users
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: The runway ignores all future income (forecast.ts:196-213). The date fell within 30 days in 99.9 percent of seeds cases, yet the real balance reached zero in only 20.8 percent. The message copy says "Your money should last until {{date}}" and "money lasts until {{date}}" (en.json:666, 680), which a user will read as a prediction that their account hits zero then. It also makes real validation harder: the quantity is not observable and must be reconstructed by removing income from statements.
  • Recommendation: Name it for what it is ("Money you have now lasts until"), and track a second, observable target (real shortfall before next money) as the primary accuracy measure in a pilot.
  • Effort: S
DATA-08 Short over-warns, especially for Ayanda
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: When Short was shown, 33 percent of cases were covered in hindsight and the real balance hit zero before next money in only 53.1 percent (Ayanda: Short right 51.6 percent). Background income (card sales, cash fares) counts toward the scrubber (forecast.ts:242-245) but not toward state.
  • Recommendation: Include a conservative share of background income (for example its 20th percentile) in the state decision, and measure alarm precision as a product metric to avoid alarm fatigue.
  • Effort: S
DATA-09 No accuracy regression test or field measurement plan in the product
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: 66 unit tests cover rules (docs/review/prep/checks.md); none measures accuracy. Nothing stores past forecasts to compare with outcomes.
  • Recommendation: Add this harness to CI with thresholds (MAE, coverage by lead time, P(short | Enough)) so engine changes cannot silently regress. In any pilot, store each shown forecast and score it against the statement 30 days later.
  • Effort: S
DATA-10 Day 0 is treated inconsistently
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Income dated today is assumed to be in the balance and next money is searched from tomorrow (forecast.ts:106, 124), but a full day of spend is subtracted for today (forecast.ts:205-209). If a payout lands today but the balance feed lags, next money skips to next week. In the first backtest run, counting day 0 income as next money moved next money accuracy for weekday personas from about 87 to 100 percent exact.
  • Recommendation: Use the timestamp of the balance and include same-day credits that are not yet in it; subtract only the rest of today's expected spend.
  • Effort: S

Real-data validation that is still needed

  1. Data: consented, de-identified transaction histories of at least 120 days from irregular earners, sourced through a bank partner or user CSV imports with explicit research consent (POPIA review by the security and legal reviewers applies). Stratify by income type (weekly platform payout, weekend trade, lumpy cash deposits, grants, mixed). Assumption: a few hundred users per stratum would give intervals of about plus or minus 5 points on rates near 10 percent; the exact number needs a power calculation once the target rates are set.
  2. Rolling-origin evaluation: many cut-offs per user, as here, with the same metrics, but scored on the observable target (real shortfall before next money, overdraft or declined debit order) as well as the reconstructed runway.
  3. Real-data issues the generator never produces: pending and reversed transactions, transfers between own accounts, cash withdrawals that hide where money went, cash income never deposited, stokvel contributions and payouts, family transfers, month-end and festive season seasonality, debit orders that move or bounce.
  4. Baselines: naive mean outflow and the bank's existing balance alert logic. The engine should beat both by a pre-registered margin.
  5. Pre-registered acceptance thresholds before a pilot, for example P(short | Enough) under 3 percent, band coverage within 10 points of the stated level at every lead time, Short precision over 70 percent. These targets are Assumptions for discussion, not sourced standards.
  6. Prospective pilot: log every forecast shown and score it 30 days later; check whether users who see Tight or Short change behaviour, since that changes the outcome the forecast is scored on.

Top three risks

  1. False reassurance for the most vulnerable pattern. A lumpy earner shown Enough runs short about 1 time in 9 on the engine's own data (1 in 4 at the demo balance level), and the copy says "even on a heavy week". Real data will be harsher.
  2. Accuracy claims built on circular evidence. The only accuracy evidence is synthetic data shaped like the engine's assumptions, with one distinct path for Ayanda in the shipped generator. A judge or bank who asks "how accurate is it on real statements" has no answer yet.
  3. Short imports degrade silently. 14 to 28 days of history, which the import allows, hides rent and other monthly bills and gives an optimistic date by several days, with a low-confidence label that does not convey the direction of the error.

Score

5/10: an accurate and well-built engine on its own synthetic world, with clear structural calibration and state-risk weaknesses and no real-data validation yet.

Sources

None. All figures come from the backtest in docs/review/backtest/ run on 2026-10-20 against the code in this repository.

4.7UI and UX designer

Reviewer file: docs/review/ux.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

Tengo is visually one of the more finished hackathon builds I would expect to see: a calm, consistent card system, a date that genuinely leads the Home screen, state shown by colour, word, icon and bar count together, careful reduced-motion handling and good empty and error states. The weakness is not polish but coherence of the numbers. On a single Home screen a user can meet three different dates (cover date, next money, "safe to spend until") and two verdicts that disagree (the hero says Short, the chart says Enough), and a Short user is told R310 is "safe to spend today" while Plan says R0 is safe to lend. The headline label also says "Enough until" when the state is Tight, which is the first thing a judge sees on the landing page. These are design and copy problems that undercut the one promise the product makes, and they are cheap to fix. In the light theme Tight and Short use almost the same colour. Fix the number story on Home and Plan before adding anything else.

What works

  • Date-first hierarchy is real. The cover date is the largest element on Home at every width (56px phone, 72 to 88px desktop, src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:22), with state chip and plain sentence directly beneath. Evidence: docs/review/screens/ux/ayanda-home-fold-light-390.png, thabo-home-light-1280.png.
  • State is never colour alone. StateChip renders icon plus word plus colour (src/components/StateChip.tsx:13), and CoverBars adds a 3, 2, 1 bar count. The 30 day chart caption explains line, shading and dot types in words, and "Show numbers" gives a table alternative (src/components/forecast/TimelineScrubber.tsx:214).
  • Motion is restrained and respects reduced motion. Global override at src/styles/index.css:123, count-up skips under reduced motion (src/lib/useCountUp.ts:8), scroll reveal never hides above-the-fold content (src/components/Reveal.tsx:15).
  • Empty and error states are designed, not default. Forecast paused has a clear cause and two actions (docs/review/screens/ux/paused-dark-390.png); a bad CSV shows a specific fix (docs/review/screens/ux/thabo-import-badfile-light-1280.png); a negative what-if amount gets inline help with an example (docs/review/screens/ux/ayanda-plan-negative-light-1280.png); render crashes have a branded fallback (src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx:22); Home with no transactions routes to import (src/pages/Home.tsx:97).
  • Honest demo labelling is consistent. "Demo | Fictional Data" chip on every app screen, simulated banners on Inbox, Privacy and Consent, a placeholder warning on the Information Officer card. Evidence: thabo-inbox-dark-1280.png, ayanda-privacy-dark-1280.png.
  • Dark and light parity in layout is complete. Every page has identical structure across themes; tokens are defined once per theme (src/styles/index.css:13 to 48) and 116 of 116 contrast pairs pass (docs/review/prep/checks.md, npm run contrast). axe passes on all audited pages (docs/review/prep/playwright.txt, 221 passed).
  • Desktop layout earns its width. At 1280 Home becomes a two-column dashboard with hero and chart left, stats, help and activity right (src/pages/Home.tsx:116); Plan puts What-if and Loan check side by side. The sidebar, persona switcher and Ctrl K menu are well placed.
  • The Why drawer explains the date in plain words with the inputs listed (balance, usual daily spend, bills) and a route to question the result (ayanda-why-drawer-light-390.png).
  • Microcopy is mostly short and human: "Covered on a normal week, not on a heavy one.", "Runs out 2 days before your next money.", "Test a change before you make it. Nothing is saved." (src/i18n/locales/en.json stateCard).

What to improve

UX-01. Short state sits next to "R310 safe to spend today" and "R0 safe to lend" - Severity: High - Evidence: Ayanda Home: hero "Short from Fri 23 Oct", "Runs out 2 days before your next money", then card "SAFE TO SPEND TODAY R310. Until Mon 26 Oct. No bills due." (docs/review/screens/ux/ayanda-home-fold-light-390.png). Her usual daily spend is R623 (ayanda-why-drawer-light-390.png), and What helps most says "Spend about R250 less a day", which implies about R373, not R310. On Plan the same person sees "SAFE TO LEND UP TO R0. You are already short" (ayanda-plan-light-390.png). The figures come from different rules (docs/review/prep/fact-sheet.md, "Safe to spend today") and the screen does not reconcile them. A Short user can read R310 as permission. - Recommendation: When state is Short, relabel the card ("Daily limit to reach your next money" or "To get through to Sun 25 Oct, keep to R310 a day"), show it beside usual spend ("You usually spend R623"), and use the same base in What helps most. Make Plan's lend wording consistent with that figure. - Effort: S

UX-02. The chart says Enough on the same screen where the hero says Short or Tight - Severity: High - Evidence: Ayanda hero "Short" while the Next 30 days readout shows a green "Enough" for Today (ayanda-home-light-390.png, ayanda-home-dark-1280.png); Thabo hero "Tight", chart "Enough" (thabo-home-dark-768.png). The readout uses day.state from a projection that includes expected income (src/components/forecast/TimelineScrubber.tsx:110). The qualifier "If money lands as usual" is 12px subtle text in the card corner (TimelineScrubber.tsx:95). - Recommendation: Do not show a state word on the chart readout for today. Show balance only, or label it "If your money lands as usual: Enough" in the readout itself. Better, show both paths on the chart (with and without the next income) so the hero's logic is visible. - Effort: S

UX-03. Headline label says "Enough until" when the state is Tight - Severity: High - Evidence: src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:9 uses hero.enoughUntil for every non-Short state. Thabo reads "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" beside an amber "Tight" chip (thabo-home-light-1280.png), and this is the product mock on the landing page hero (landing-light-1280.png, landing-dark-390.png). Plan repeats it in the Now and After cards (thabo-plan-dark-1280.png). The Inbox message already words it better: "should last until Tue 27 Oct on a normal week. Cover: Tight." (thabo-inbox-dark-1280.png). - Recommendation: Use a state-specific label: Enough "Covered until", Tight "Lasts until (on a normal week)", Short "Short from". Keep the Inbox phrasing as the model. - Effort: S

UX-04. Three different dates for "next money" on one screen - Severity: Medium - Evidence: Ayanda: hero "Short from Fri 23 Oct", band "Next money around Sun 25 Oct", safe to spend "Until Mon 26 Oct" (ux/ayanda-home-fold-light-390.png). Lerato: next money Sat 24 Oct, safe to spend "Until Sun 25 Oct" (lerato-home-dark-390.png). Cause: Stats uses nextMoney.latest for the safe-to-spend date and nextMoney.typical for the Next money figure (src/pages/Home.tsx:56, 74). - Recommendation: Pick one date the user sees (typical) and explain the buffer in words ("We plan to Mon 26 Oct in case your money is a day late"), or show next money as a range everywhere. - Effort: S

UX-05. Tight and Short are nearly the same colour in the light theme - Severity: Medium - Evidence: Light tokens --tight: 155 71 8 and --short: 169 57 10 (src/styles/index.css:47, 48): contrast ratio between them 1.01 (computed with the WCAG luminance formula), both read as rust brown. Compare picker chips (picker-light-390.png) and Me (lerato-me-light-390.png). In dark they are clearly amber versus coral. Icon, word and bar count still differ, so this is not a WCAG failure, but the colour signal for the most important distinction is lost in light. - Recommendation: Move light Tight towards a true amber or ochre (for example a darker yellow-brown with higher luminance on the soft fill) and keep Short as a red. Re-run the contrast script for text on soft fills. - Effort: S

UX-06. Plan opens with a pre-filled R500 what-if and a R300 loan - Severity: Medium - Evidence: src/pages/Plan.tsx:59 (amount: '500') and :187 (useState('300')). The page loads already showing "2 days less" and "Short from Sun 25 Oct" for Thabo (thabo-plan-dark-1280.png) before he has done anything. For Ayanda the What-if says "1 day less" on arrival (ayanda-plan-light-390.png). A first-time user may think something has happened to their money. On Thabo's Loan check "Use R320" is styled as selected while the field holds 300. - Recommendation: Start empty with a neutral result ("Add an amount to see your date move"), and keep the quick chips. If a demo needs a filled state, use the placeholder or a "Try R500" chip. - Effort: S

UX-07. The range band has no visible legend for its main marker, and the headline date can sit at the edge of "likely" - Severity: Medium - Evidence: src/components/forecast/RangeBand.tsx:29 to 48 draws band, a state-coloured dot (the headline date) and an accent pin (next money); only the pin has a legend line (:49). The whole graphic is aria-hidden. Lerato's date 1 Nov sits on the far right edge of "Likely between Mon 26 Oct and Sun 01 Nov" (lerato-home-dark-390.png), so "likely" and the headline look inconsistent to a lay reader. - Recommendation: Label the dot ("Your date") and the band ("Likely range") directly on the track, and explain in the Why drawer why the headline can sit at one end of the range. - Effort: S

UX-08. The 30 day chart scale is driven by the optimistic band, so the balance line hugs zero - Severity: Medium - Evidence: TimelineScrubber.tsx:59, 60: y-axis max is the highest optimistic value. Ayanda's axis runs R30k to minus R10k while her balance is about R1.3k, so the expected line is squashed into the bottom fifth of the plot and the shading dips well below R0 (ayanda-home-light-390.png). Below-zero shading on a "money lasts" chart looks like debt. - Recommendation: Scale to the central line plus a capped band (for example 80th percentile), clip shading at R0 or tint it differently, and mark the cover date on the chart so it ties back to the hero. - Effort: M

UX-09. Daily messages is switched on by default at consent - Severity: Medium - Evidence: src/pages/Consent.tsx:27 useState(true); the toggle shows "On" before the user chooses (thabo-consent-dark-390.png). Analytics is off by default, so the pattern is inconsistent, and a pre-ticked optional choice reads as a nudge. - Recommendation: Default Daily messages to off and offer it as a positive choice after the first Home view ("Want this date each morning on WhatsApp?"). - Effort: S

UX-10. Import step 2 is dense and its summary tiles are ambiguous - Severity: Low - Evidence: After "Use sample statement" the phone shows six column dropdowns, a preview table without an amount column, then tiles "DAYS 45, INCOME 14, SPEND 37, BILL 2" (thabo-import-sample-light-390.png). "INCOME 14" reads as R14, not 14 transactions. The column mapping is shown even when every guess is right. - Recommendation: Collapse the mapping behind "Columns look wrong?" when detection succeeds, add the amount column to the preview, and relabel tiles ("14 money-in rows"). - Effort: S

UX-11. Microcopy and format inconsistencies - Severity: Low - Evidence: "Pay Car instalment (R3 450) after Tue 27 Oct instead of before." capitalises the bill mid-sentence (thabo-home-light-1280.png, en.json:697 inserts the raw description). "To reach Tight" frames Tight as a goal (ayanda-home-dark-1280.png, en.json:704). Lerato sees "safe to spend R330" and "You could set aside up to R280 today" on the same screen (lerato-home-dark-390.png). Dates show "Sun 01 Nov" and "Sept" from the en-ZA locale, while the native date input shows "2026/10/21" (thabo-plan-dark-1280.png). - Recommendation: Lower-case bill names in sentences, reword the help goal ("To avoid running short" or "To be covered on a heavy week"), explain the R330 versus R280 difference or show one figure, and format days without a leading zero. - Effort: S

UX-12. Phone first fold and tablet layout - Severity: Low - Evidence: At 390x844 the hero fills the fold and the safe-to-spend card is cut by the bottom bar, with Next money and Confidence hidden (ux/ayanda-home-fold-light-390.png). At 768 the layout is the phone stack stretched wide, with a greeting banner taking prime space above the hero (thabo-home-dark-768.png). Privacy is only an unlabelled shield icon in the top bar on phones (src/layouts/nav.ts, any *-390.png). - Recommendation: Tighten hero padding on phones so the safe-to-spend figure is visible without scrolling; fold the greeting into a single line on tablet; use the two-column layout from 768; give the shield a visible label or put Privacy under Me. - Effort: M

UX-13. Persona lists truncate key context and show a fixed starting state - Severity: Low - Evidence: Me truncates "E-hailing driver, Tsh..." and "Spaza shop owner, ..." (lerato-me-light-390.png). Picker and Me chips show the designed startState, not the current one (src/pages/Picker.tsx, docs/review/prep/walkthrough.md item 6). - Recommendation: Allow two lines for the role, and compute the chip from the current forecast or drop it. - Effort: S

Nielsen heuristic evaluation

Scale: 1 (serious problems) to 5 (excellent).

# Heuristic Score Evidence
1 Visibility of system status 4 Demo chip on every screen, "Last changed" on consent, Plan result badges ("2 days less", "No change"), import row counts and skipped-row warning (thabo-import-sample-light-390.png). Count-up and re-keyed date animate on change (DateHero.tsx:19).
2 Match with the real world 3 Plain language ("next money", "a heavy week") fits the personas. Undercut by "Enough until" for Tight (UX-03), "To reach Tight" (UX-11) and "Income 14" counts (UX-10).
3 User control and freedom 4 Remove what-if rows, Not now on consent, Resume forecast, Cancel on import, reset demo data, persona switch everywhere. Pre-filled Plan values add changes the user did not ask for (UX-06).
4 Consistency and standards 2 Same card system and chips across pages, but the numbers are inconsistent: three next-money dates (UX-04), chart state versus hero state (UX-02), R310 versus R0 versus R373 (UX-01), R330 versus R280 (UX-11), Tight and Short colours in light (UX-05).
5 Error prevention 3 Import refuses under 14 days and asks for column mapping; delete needs a checkbox. Consent pre-enables messages (UX-09); a Short user is shown a spendable figure without a caveat (UX-01).
6 Recognition rather than recall 4 Labelled bottom nav and sidebar, chart caption explains marks, Why drawer lists inputs. Range band marker unlabelled (UX-07); Privacy is an unlabelled icon on phones (UX-12).
7 Flexibility and efficiency 4 Ctrl or Cmd K menu, quick amount chips, filters and search on Money, chart scrubbing plus table view.
8 Aesthetic and minimalist design 4 Clean hierarchy and generous spacing. Import step 2 and the five-card consent screen are long on phones (thabo-consent-dark-390.png).
9 Help users recognise, diagnose and recover from errors 4 Specific inline messages with examples (ux/ayanda-plan-negative-light-1280.png, ux/thabo-import-badfile-light-1280.png), paused state with actions, crash fallback.
10 Help and documentation 4 Why this date drawer, "How the forecast works" in Me, "What the file needs" on import. No help for what Tight means beyond one sentence, and nothing explains why safe to spend differs from usual spend.

Average 3.6 of 5. The low point is consistency of the numbers, which is the heuristic that matters most for a money app.

Cognitive walkthrough: first two minutes

Thabo (e-hailing driver, Tight). - 0 to 20 s: Landing shows "Money you can plan around" and a mock with his own figures. He must understand that the date is when money runs out. Risk: the mock says "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" next to "Tight" (UX-03); he may read Enough as the verdict. - 20 to 50 s: Picker, then a consent page several screens tall on a phone (thabo-consent-dark-390.png). He must understand he can agree. Risk: Daily messages already on (UX-09); he may scroll past without reading. - 50 to 90 s: Home. He sees Tue 27 Oct, Tight, "Covered on a normal week, not on a heavy one." This works. Next he sees R295 safe to spend "Until Tue 27 Oct" but Next money Mon 26 Oct (UX-04), then a chart saying Enough (UX-02). He must work out which one to believe. - 90 to 120 s: What helps most says "Pay Car instalment after Tue 27 Oct". He may not know if this is allowed, and the bill shows as due Sun 25 Oct on Money (thabo-money-light-1280.png). Tapping Try it in Plan lands on a page already showing "2 days less" and Short (UX-06); he may think his position just got worse.

Lerato (hairdresser, Enough). - 0 to 40 s: Picker shows her as Enough with three green bars; consent as above. - 40 to 90 s: Home shows Enough until Sun 01 Nov, green chip, "Covered until your next money, even on a heavy week." Clear and reassuring. Risk: next money is Sat 24 Oct, so she may wonder why the date is 1 Nov and why the dot sits at the very end of "Likely between Mon 26 Oct and Sun 01 Nov" (UX-07). - 90 to 120 s: R330 safe to spend "Until Sun 25 Oct", then "You could set aside up to R280 today" (UX-11). She must decide if she can spend R330 and save R280, or only one of them. Nothing on the screen answers that.

Ayanda (spaza owner, Short). - 0 to 40 s: Picker shows Short with one bar. Consent as above. - 40 to 70 s: Home shows Short from Fri 23 Oct and "Runs out 2 days before your next money." This is the clearest moment in the product: the warning is unmissable. - 70 to 100 s: Directly below: "SAFE TO SPEND TODAY R310. No bills due." (UX-01). She must understand this is a ceiling to stretch money to Mon 26 Oct, not a free amount, while she usually spends R623. Likely misreading: "I am short, but I can still spend R310 today." - 100 to 120 s: Scrolling, the chart shows a green "Enough" for today (UX-02) with an axis up to R30k (UX-08). She may conclude the warning was a false alarm. If she opens Plan, the loan card says R0 is safe to lend and the what-if already shows "1 day less" from a R500 she did not enter (UX-06).

Top three risks

  1. False reassurance for the Short user. R310 safe to spend plus a green Enough on the chart can override the Short headline for exactly the person who most needs the warning (UX-01, UX-02).
  2. Loss of trust from contradictory numbers. Three next-money dates and two state verdicts on one screen make the forecast look unreliable to judges and users even where the engine is correct (UX-03, UX-04).
  3. First impression on the landing page. The hero mock shows "Enough until" beside "Tight", so the core concept is muddled in the first five seconds of the demo (UX-03).

Score

6 out of 10: polished, accessible and consistent visual design, held back by Home and Plan figures that contradict each other in ways a stressed user could act on.

Sources

None.

4.8Accessibility and inclusive design specialist

Reviewer file: docs/review/accessibility.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

Tengo has a stronger accessibility base than most hackathon builds. axe-core 4.14 with the WCAG 2.2 AA tags finds one violation across 15 pages and 4 overlays in both themes at 390 and 1280 px. Nothing scrolls sideways at 320 CSS px, every target is at least 24 px, reduced motion is honoured in CSS and in JavaScript, and the drawer and command menu follow the expected keyboard patterns. The tools miss the problems that matter for this audience. When a user picks Tshivenda, Sepedi or any other language, the page declares that language while every word is still English (WCAG 3.1.1). The chart slider tells a screen reader "Enough" for a person whose headline says "Short", and it hides the bills and income that explain each jump. Keyboard focus goes behind the fixed bottom tab bar on phones (2.4.11). The app shows a blank page offline after a first visit, which is when loadshedding is most likely to catch a new user. Beyond WCAG, the Home screen asks people who may be anxious about numbers to reconcile about 20 figures that sometimes contradict each other. Nothing protects private money data on a shared phone. This is a careful build with a few high-impact gaps, all fixable in days rather than weeks.

What works

  • Automated scan is nearly clean. My own axe run (tags wcag2a, wcag2aa, wcag21a, wcag21aa, wcag22aa) over 15 routes plus the Why drawer, chart table, command menu and a Tshivenda session, in dark and light at 390 and 1280 px, found 1 violation: the chart table scroll region, A11Y-08. Evidence: docs/review/tools/accessibility/results/axe-{dark,light}-{390,1280}.json, script docs/review/tools/accessibility/axe.a11y.ts. The "incomplete" contrast items are SVG axis text and labels over translucent bars. The shipped token pairs pass (docs/review/prep/checks.md: 116 of 116 pairs).
  • Reflow at 320 CSS px (1.4.10). On 12 routes at 320 x 640, scrollWidth equals the viewport width and no text runs off screen. Evidence: results/manual.json key reflow320. 320 px is the 400 percent equivalent of a 1280 px window. Nothing blocks zoom: no maximum-scale or user-scalable in index.html, and the manifest sets no orientation lock (1.3.4).
  • Target size (2.5.8). Every control measured at 320 px is at least 24 x 24, and most are 44 px tall. The smallest are the 28 px privacy switch labels, which also have a 44 px input. Evidence: results/manual.json reflow320.*.small.
  • Dragging alternative (2.5.7). The chart is a native input type=range laid over the plot (src/components/forecast/TimelineScrubber.tsx:190). A single tap sets the day (my tap at 60 percent set value 16, results/manual.json chart.tapSetsValue). Arrow keys, Home and End work, and "Show numbers" gives a full table. touch-action: pan-y (src/styles/index.css:198) keeps vertical page scroll working on phones.
  • Overlays behave. The Why drawer moves focus to Close, keeps Tab inside the dialog, closes on Escape and returns focus to "Why this date" (results/manual.json drawer, src/components/Drawer.tsx:24). The command menu follows the combobox pattern. Typing "plan" then Enter navigates and moves focus to main. A theme command is announced in the live region ("Light theme on"). Evidence: results/command-menu.json.
  • Screen reader basics are thoughtful. The date hero reads "Short from Friday 23 October" as an h1, with the short visual date hidden (src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:16). Amounts read "money in, R204". The count-up figure has a static sr-only copy (src/pages/Home.tsx:61). State is shown with colour, word and icon (src/components/StateChip.tsx:12, 1.4.1). Route changes move focus to #main and announce the page title (src/components/A11y.tsx:44). There is a skip link.
  • Reduced motion. A global CSS override (src/styles/index.css:123), and useCountUp and Reveal both check prefers-reduced-motion (src/lib/useCountUp.ts:8, src/components/Reveal.tsx:14).
  • Language plumbing is ready. Each language option carries its own lang (src/components/LanguageSelector.tsx:46, 3.1.2). Inbox messages carry lang (src/pages/Inbox.tsx:117). Headings switch to Inter for Tshivenda because Figtree lacks ḓ ḽ ṅ ṋ ṱ (src/styles/index.css:84).
  • Plain English. A rough Flesch-Kincaid estimate over all 565 English strings gives about grade 3. Plan and Help sections score under grade 2 and the Why explanation about grade 2. This is a heuristic on short UI strings and tends to underestimate difficulty. Form errors give an example, for instance "Enter an amount above zero, for example 250." (src/i18n/locales/en.json:532, 3.3.3).
  • Data cost is small. A cold Home load transfers 149 KB (docs/review/prep/perf-home.json). At ICASA's reported average of about R71 for a 1 GB prepaid bundle, that is roughly R0.01 per cold load.

What to improve

A11Y-01 Page language says Tshivenda or Sepedi while the text is English
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: src/components/Preferences.tsx:12 sets document.documentElement.lang = language for any choice, but src/i18n/status.json marks all 10 non-English languages "pending" and they render English. Measured: with ve, nso or zu selected, html lang is ve, nso or zu and the h1 is "Short from Friday 23 October" (results/manual.json key lang). This fails WCAG 3.1.1 Language of Page because the declared language is wrong. A screen reader with a matching voice installed, or a translate prompt, will treat English as Tshivenda. axe cannot detect this, and the Tshivenda scan passes.
  • Recommendation: Set lang to the language actually rendered. Use en while a locale is pending, and when a locale is partly translated, put lang="en" on any string that falls back to English. Add a Playwright check that the html lang value matches the status.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-02 Blank page offline after a first visit (loadshedding)
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: public/sw.js precaches only /, the manifest and two icons. The hashed JS and CSS are cached only when fetched while the worker is in control, and on a first visit they load before it takes control. Test: one visit, worker "activated", cache holds /, manifest, icons and two fonts but no JS or CSS. After going offline and reloading, the page has no h1 and an empty body (results/offline-single-visit.json, script offline.a11y.ts). The repo's own test passes only because it reloads once while online before going offline (tests/flows.spec.ts:131). There is no offline message.
  • Recommendation: Precache the build output at install (Workbox or vite-plugin-pwa injecting the asset manifest). Add a static offline fallback that says in plain words that the app needs one connection to finish setting up. Change the test to go offline straight after the first visit.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-03 The chart slider tells screen reader users something different from the headline and hides what moves the balance
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: For Ayanda, the h1 reads "Short from Friday 23 October", while the slider's first value reads "Tuesday 20 October, about R1 312, Enough" (results/manual.json chart.ayanda). The "If money lands as usual" caveat sits only in the section description, not in the value text (src/i18n/locales/en.json:389). Income and bill notes for the chosen day are aria-hidden (TimelineScrubber.tsx:118) and absent from valueText (TimelineScrubber.tsx:79). So Thabo's balance falls to R971 on Sunday and rises to R3 643 on Monday with no spoken reason. Sighted users see "Expected money +R2 860". The likely range is also visual only. Information shown visually is not exposed programmatically (1.3.1). For a blind user the unexplained "Enough" is a false reassurance risk.
  • Recommendation: Put events and assumptions into aria-valuetext, for example ", about , Enough if money lands as usual. due, minus . Likely to ." Give the slider a name that includes "if money lands as usual". Consider showing the day state as "Covered if paid" so the chart and the headline do not use the same word for different rules.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-04 Keyboard focus hidden behind the fixed bottom tab bar
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: At 390 x 844, tabbing to "Try it in Plan" on Home and to "Edit Paraffin" on Money leaves the focused control under the tab bar. The bar's top edge is at 783 px, the controls sit at 789 to 844 px, and elementFromPoint returns the tab bar (results/focus-obscured-app-390.png, results/focus-obscured-app-money-390.png). Seven transaction edit buttons on Money were fully hidden (results/manual.json obscured-/app/money). This fails WCAG 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured (Minimum). The bar is defined in src/layouts/AppLayout.tsx:166 and the codebase has no scroll-padding.
  • Recommendation: Add html { scroll-padding-top: 64px; scroll-padding-bottom: calc(76px + env(safe-area-inset-bottom)); } below the lg breakpoint. Add a Playwright check that the focused element is not covered.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-05 Too many numbers, some of them contradicting each other, for people anxious about money
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: Ayanda's first phone screen shows "Short", "Runs out 2 days before your next money", "Safe to spend today R310" (her usual daily spend is R623), and a chart readout of "R1 312 ... Enough". The chart axis runs from minus R10k to R30k, and the range on the last chart day is "Likely -R1 882 to R21 173" (docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-light-390.png, results/manual.json chart.ayanda.main). Above "What helps most" I count about 20 distinct figures. Not a WCAG failure. For readers with low numeracy, a red "Short" next to a confident "R310 safe" invites them to keep the number they would rather believe.
  • Recommendation: Lead with one sentence and one action, for example "Your money runs out on Friday, 2 days before Sunday's money. Spend about R250 less a day to close the gap." Move the range, confidence and 30 day chart behind "See the detail". Reconcile safe to spend with Short, or label it "only if you also cut R250 a day". Test with 5 users scoring low on a numeracy screener.
  • Effort: M
A11Y-06 No privacy on a shared phone
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: Full transaction history, balance, consent and audit log sit in plain localStorage, 16.5 KB for one persona (results/manual.json storage, src/data/localRepository.ts:15). There is no PIN, no "hide amounts" mode and no sign-out, and the session reopens straight to Home. The planned messages contain the date and cover state (src/i18n/locales/en.json:666), which would appear on a lock screen. Assumption: phone sharing within households is common among lower-income South African users. I did not find a current sourced figure, so the range is wide: 10 to 40 percent of the target segment.
  • Recommendation: Before any pilot, add an optional 4 digit app lock or device biometric, a one tap "hide amounts" toggle, and a discreet message mode with no amounts or state on the lock screen ("Tengo has an update"). Make sure the lock uses no cognitive test and allows paste and password managers (3.3.8 Accessible Authentication).
  • Effort: M
A11Y-07 Form fields and chart shading below 3:1 non-text contrast
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: .field uses border-line on bg-surface. The border token gives 1.24:1 against surface in dark and 1.26:1 in light, and the field fill is the same colour as the card (src/styles/index.css:16,40, my calculation from the tokens). The border is the only cue that identifies the input, so it needs 3:1 under 1.4.11. The chart's likely-range band is 1.25:1 to 1.27:1 against the card. The band is supplementary because the table and readout carry the range, so it matters less. The repo's contrast check covers text pairs only (docs/review/prep/checks.md).
  • Recommendation: Give inputs a border-strong that reaches 3:1, for example around rgb(110 110 118) in dark and rgb(140 136 128) in light, and verify with the contrast script. Add non-text pairs (field border, switch track, focus ring) to scripts/contrast-check.mjs.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-08 Chart data table cannot be scrolled by keyboard
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: axe scrollable-region-focusable (serious, WCAG 2.1.1) on the max-h-[360px] overflow-auto table container in all four runs (results/axe-*-*.json, overlay chart-table; TimelineScrubber.tsx:224). The table has no focusable content, so keyboard users cannot reach days below the fold. The table is also wider than a 390 px phone (min-w-[460px]).
  • Recommendation: Add tabIndex={0}, role="region" and an aria-label to the container, as the drawer body already does (Drawer.tsx:79). On phones, show 2 columns (day, expected) and put the range on a second line.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-09 The multilingual, low-literacy audience gets English only
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: 10 of 11 locales are English copies (src/i18n/status.json). PIRLS 2021 found 81 percent of South African Grade 4 learners could not read for meaning in any language (Daily Maverick, source below), so reading load matters for adults in the target segment too. Most strings are short, but some privacy and Me strings estimate at grade 9 to 11 (me.sim.translations, privacy.breach.contact.body, privacy.complaints). Nothing is available as audio or voice.
  • Recommendation: Before claiming multilingual reach, ship 2 or 3 languages that match the personas' areas (for example isiZulu, Sepedi, Setswana), each reviewed by a first-language speaker. Add a "read this to me" button on the date hero using the device's speech synthesis where a voice exists. Rewrite the privacy strings to grade 5 or lower.
  • Effort: L
A11Y-10 Import errors are not explained or announced
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: "This file could not be read as a CSV." (en.json:582) gives no next step (3.3.3), and the Notice that shows it has no live role (src/components/Feedback.tsx:54, src/pages/Import.tsx:131), so screen reader users are not told (4.1.3). An invalid balance sets aria-invalid with no error text and only disables the Save button (src/pages/Import.tsx:248, 3.3.1).
  • Recommendation: Say what to do ("Save the statement from your banking app as CSV, or try the sample"). Render errors with role="alert". Add a visible error under the balance field and keep the button enabled, so pressing it explains the problem.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-11 Negative amounts may be read without "minus"
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Spoken text uses a hyphen-minus, for example "Friday 23 October, about -R384, Short" (results/manual.json chart.ayanda.slider, src/lib/format.ts:13). Assumption: at default punctuation levels some screen readers and TalkBack voices skip or misread a leading hyphen. I did not test with a real screen reader, so this needs checking on NVDA and TalkBack.
  • Recommendation: In aria-valuetext and sr-only copies, use words: "R384 below zero" or "minus R384".
  • Effort: S
A11Y-12 Truncated text loses meaning at small widths and high zoom
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Transaction names and persona roles are cut with an ellipsis: "Cash deposit, shop takin…" at 390 px, "E-hailing …" and "Spaza s…" at 320 px (docs/review/screens/ayanda-home-light-390.png, results/home-320.png from the Me page). Screen readers get the full text, so this is not a WCAG failure, but sighted low-literacy users lose the words they would recognise.
  • Recommendation: Allow 2 lines (line-clamp-2) instead of truncate for descriptions and roles.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-13 Command menu is keyboard-only and its groups are not announced
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Below the lg breakpoint there is no visible control to open it (results/manual.json commandMenu.mobileOpenButtonVisible: 0, opener only in the sidebar, AppLayout.tsx:89). Group headings are <p role="presentation"> inside role="presentation" items (CommandPalette.tsx:197), so "Pages", "People" and "Language" are not announced. Ctrl+K also opens it from inside a text field (results/command-menu.json opensFromTextField: 1), which overrides a common editing shortcut in some browsers.
  • Recommendation: Use role="group" with aria-labelledby for each section. Ignore the shortcut when focus is in an editable field, unless the field is empty. On phones this is optional, because everything is reachable in Me.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-14 Low-end Android parses translations that are not there yet
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: One 696 KB JS file (142 KB gzip), of which 337 KB is locale JSON for 11 languages, 10 of them English copies (docs/review/prep/audit.md). Cold hero at 4x CPU slowdown is 1.5 s (perf-home.json), which is acceptable. Assumption: entry-level Android phones with 1 to 2 GB RAM and older CPUs are 2 to 4 times slower than the 4x throttle, which would put the cold hero at 2 to 4 s.
  • Recommendation: Load only the active locale on demand, and cache it in the service worker. Test once on a real entry-level Android Go device.
  • Effort: S
A11Y-15 Before a pilot: authentication, help and accessibility testing with people
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: There is no login yet, so 3.3.8 Accessible Authentication does not apply today. The privacy pages have a "Question this result" help route, but nothing appears in the same place on every page (3.2.6 Consistent Help applies once a real help channel exists). No test with screen reader users or low-literacy users is recorded (docs/review/prep/walkthrough.md is a developer walkthrough).
  • Recommendation: Design the bank-grade login to allow biometrics, paste and password managers, with no puzzles. Put one "Help" link in the same place on every page. Run a session with 3 TalkBack users and 5 low-literacy users before a pilot.
  • Effort: M

Top three risks

  1. Misleading reassurance for screen reader users. The slider says "Enough" while the headline says "Short", and the bills that explain the drops are hidden from assistive tech (A11Y-03). A blind user could act on the wrong signal.
  2. The app fails exactly when power and network drop. After one visit it shows a blank page offline (A11Y-02), and the first visit is the moment a new user is most likely to lose signal during loadshedding.
  3. Multilingual claim versus reality. Picking a language changes lang but not the words (A11Y-01, A11Y-09). A judge or user who picks isiZulu sees English, and assistive tech gets the wrong language, which undercuts the inclusion pitch.

Score

6/10: a carefully built accessible base with near-clean automated results, held back by a language-of-page failure, offline failure on first visit, contradictory screen reader output on the chart, focus hidden under the tab bar, and a Home screen too dense in numbers for its audience.

  • Daily Maverick, "International study shows 81% of Grade 4s in South Africa cannot read for meaning" (PIRLS 2021), https://www.dailymaverick.co.za/article/2023-05-16-international-study-shows-81-of-grade-4s-in-south-africa-cannot-read-for-meaning/ (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • ICASA, Bi-annual Tariff Analysis Report 2025/26 Q2 (abridged), average price of about R71 for 1 GB to 1.2 GB, https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/2025-26FY-Q2-Bi-annual-Tariff-Analysis-Report-Abridged-Report-For-Publication.pdf (accessed 2026-10-20)

Tools and raw outputs: docs/review/tools/accessibility/ (axe.a11y.ts, manual.a11y.ts, cmd.a11y.ts, obscured.a11y.ts, offline.a11y.ts, a11y.config.ts) and docs/review/tools/accessibility/results/. Run with npx playwright test -c docs/review/tools/accessibility/a11y.config.ts. The first command menu reading in manual.json was a timing artefact. command-menu.json is the corrected run.

4.9Behavioural economist and financial inclusion researcher

Reviewer file: docs/review/behavioural.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Verdict

Tengo asks the right question for people paid in lumps, and its date-first answer, daily figure and loan check are a credible attempt to turn a balance into a decision. The behavioural case is weaker than the pitch suggests. No published study shows that a runway date changes behaviour more than a balance does, so that claim is a hypothesis to test, not a finding. Three frames also carry real risk of harm. "Safe to spend today" can license spending well above a person's normal rate and drop them a level (Lerato: R330 a day against a usual R120, with rent of R2 800 due a week later and not mentioned). "Safe to lend up to" turns a ceiling into a default with a one-tap button and ignores the money already promised to safe to spend and to "set aside". The model only sees one bank account, while the people it targets live largely in cash, stokvels, family transfers and several wallets, none of which appear in the seed data or the engine. The product is promising as a prompt for reflection and not yet safe as a guide to action. It needs the frames fixed and a small field test with real earners in Tshwane, Mamelodi and Tembisa before anyone claims it builds money confidence.

What works

  • The question matches lived reality. Irregular, lumpy income is the defining feature of low-income household finance in South Africa (Collins, Morduch, Rutherford and Ruthven, Portfolios of the Poor, which included South African financial diaries). The tagline "is my money enough until the next money" names that problem in plain words. Evidence: README.md lines 1 to 9, src/i18n/locales/en.json:346-368.
  • Daily framing is well chosen for adoption. Framing a recurring amount per day rather than per month quadrupled enrolment in a savings programme and removed the income gap in take-up (Hershfield, Shu and Benartzi 2020). "Safe to spend today" uses the same unit as the day-to-day decisions of a driver, hairdresser or shop owner. Evidence: src/engine/forecast.ts (safeToSpendToday), docs/review/screens/thabo-home-light-390.png.
  • Salience of a specific upcoming cost. Reminders work best when they name a specific future lumpy expense (Karlan, McConnell, Mullainathan and Zinman 2016), and salience of overdraft fees reduced overdrafts, most among less financially literate customers (Stango and Zinman 2014). Tengo's date, "Bills before next money" and the Why this date drawer make future costs salient in the same way. Evidence: docs/review/screens/ayanda-why-drawer-light-390.png.
  • Transparent, conservative headline. The headline counts only money already in the account and says so ("Money you have not received yet is not counted"), and Enough requires the heavy week path to reach the latest likely next money. This avoids the most common reassurance error of income forecasts. Evidence: README.md:302, src/engine/forecast.ts:217, ayanda-why-drawer-light-390.png.
  • Short is paired with an action, not only a warning. Short states show "What helps most" with a concrete daily cut and a "Try it in Plan" link, and the daily message ends with "Check Plan in Tengo to see what helps most". Action-first messages reduce the chance that bad news leads to avoidance (see BEH-06). Evidence: ayanda-home-light-390.png, src/i18n/locales/en.json:674-676.
  • The loan check exists at all. Lending and giving to family is a core behaviour that most budgeting tools ignore. Showing "days less cover" before lending gives people a concrete, shareable reason for a smaller amount. Evidence: docs/review/screens/thabo-plan-dark-1280.png.
  • Privacy-aware message content. The daily message promises "Never your balance or transactions", which matters on shared phones. Evidence: docs/review/screens/ayanda-inbox-light-390.png.

What to improve

BEH-01 "Safe to spend today" can license overspending and hides the next big bill

Severity: High Evidence: Safe to spend is balance minus bills before the latest next money, divided by days to it (src/engine/forecast.ts, perDay). It is not tied to the person's spending rate or to the cover state. For Lerato it is R330 while her usual daily spend is R120 and her heavy week rate is R276 (docs/review/prep/persona-snapshot.json). My probe with the real engine (docs/review/tools/behavioural/frames.ts) shows that if Lerato spends the R330 every day until next money, she drops from Enough to Tight and her date moves from 1 Nov to 25 Oct. Her rent of R2 800 on 1 Nov is the reason her headline date is 1 Nov, yet neither the safe to spend card nor "You are covered" mentions it (lerato-home-dark-1280.png). Daily framing makes amounts feel small and acceptable (Atlas and Bartels, periodic pricing; Hershfield, Shu and Benartzi), so a daily figure framed as "safe" will be read as an allowance. A preliminary conference poster also suggests budget feedback can speed up spending towards the limit (Huang and Liang, UBC poster, treat as preliminary). Recommendation: Make safe to spend the largest daily amount that keeps the current state (the same search already used for safe to lend), never above the heavy week rate when Enough. Rename it "Spend up to R… a day to stay Enough". Always show the next large bill after next money ("Rent R2 800 on Sun 1 Nov"). Test the wording in the user study (questions C4 and C5). Effort: S

BEH-02 The model sees one bank account; the target users live in cash, wallets and several accounts

Severity: High Evidence: There is no cash ledger, no manual income or spend entry, no detection of transfers between own accounts, and no handling of cash withdrawals (grep of src/engine and src/lib finds no ATM, transfer or withdraw rule except the CSV debit column name at src/lib/csv.ts:50). A withdrawal to cash is counted as everyday spend, a transfer to an e-wallet or savings pocket as spend, and money moved in from another own account as income that can become "next money". FinScope SA Consumer 2023 reports that 94 percent withdraw cash monthly, and the SARB Payments Study found cash is 56 percent of payments by volume. The seed data understates this: Thabo's "Cash fares" are deposited into the bank most days (src/data/seed/generate.ts), which few drivers would do in practice (Assumption: from the FinScope cash withdrawal pattern, most daily cash is spent from the pocket). The persona copy says "Cash fares most days" and "Cash takings come in lumps" (src/i18n/locales/en.json:122,130), but the engine only sees cash that reaches the bank. Recommendation: Add a "Cash in hand" figure the user updates in one tap, a quick "money in" and "money out" entry, a transfer rule (same amount out and in within two days, or a user tag "my other account or wallet"), and a prompt on large deposits: "Is this your income, a stokvel payout, or your own money moved?". Show "Based on your bank account only" under the date until cash is added. Effort: M

BEH-03 "Safe to lend up to" anchors a default and double counts the same money

Severity: High Evidence: The loan check shows a large "Safe to lend up to R320" and a one-tap "Use R320" chip (src/pages/Plan.tsx:204-238, thabo-plan-dark-1280.png). A displayed maximum with a button to apply it acts as an anchor and a default. Safe to lend is computed independently of safe to spend and of "set aside": Lerato is told she can set aside R280 (Home) and lend R280 (Plan), and spend R330 today, all from the same buffer (src/engine/help.ts:49 reuses safeToLend for canSetAside). My probe shows that lending R280 and spending R330 on the same day drops Lerato to Tight, and Thabo lending R320 plus spending R295 moves his date a day earlier (frames.ts output). The loan is modelled as gone, with no option for an expected repayment date or partial repayment, and the Short message "no amount is safe to lend" is a moral verdict that people facing family obligations will override (Mangoma and Wilson-Prangley 2019 find transfers to family are substantial and usually monthly; Magubane 2017 finds black tax can crowd out savings). Positively, a number from a neutral tool can be a socially acceptable reason to give less, which is worth keeping. Recommendation: Reframe as "Give or lend without dropping a level", show one shared budget ("Today you can spend R… or lend R…, not both"), remove the "Use" chip or relabel it "Show me the most", add "Expect it back on" with a date that feeds the what-if, and in Short show the cost in days ("Lending R200 brings your date forward 1 day") instead of "no amount is safe". Add a short, optional script the user can share ("I can do R… this week, more after the 25th"). Effort: M

BEH-04 Conflicting signals undermine comprehension and trust

Severity: Medium Evidence: (1) Thabo's hero reads "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" with a "Tight" chip (src/components/forecast/DateHero.tsx:9 uses "Enough until" for every non-Short state; thabo-home-light-390.png). (2) Ayanda's hero says Short while the 30 day chart says Enough for today (ayanda-home-light-390.png, walkthrough.md item 3). (3) Ayanda gets two daily targets: safe to spend R310 and "Spend about R250 less a day", which implies about R373 against her R623 usual rate, with different end dates (26 Oct and 25 Oct) (frames.ts output, src/engine/help.ts). (4) Lerato's headline date equals the end of her range (band 26 Oct to 1 Nov, headline 1 Nov), so the "likely" date sits at the edge rather than the middle (persona-snapshot.json). People under financial strain have less attention to reconcile mixed messages (Mani, Mullainathan, Shafir and Zhao 2013; note Carvalho, Meier and Wang 2016 found no cognitive effect around payday, so the size of this effect is contested). Recommendation: Use a state-neutral label ("Money lasts until"). Give one daily number per state and make "What helps most" express its cut as that number. Reconcile the chart and hero with a visible line ("If your money lands as usual on Sun 25 Oct, you are back to Enough"). Test comprehension of the range with question C3. Effort: S

BEH-05 Business inputs are treated as household spending to cut

Severity: Medium Evidence: Ayanda's top spend is stock (R15 137 over 90 days) and "What helps most" tells her to spend R250 less a day (persona-snapshot.json, ayanda-home-light-390.png). For a spaza owner, cutting stock cuts future takings. Thabo's fuel is the input to his income. The bill-move suggestion for Thabo names his car instalment (walkthrough.md item 10), which for an e-hailing driver is the asset that earns the income. A Short state that appears every restock cycle risks alert fatigue for a shop owner whose business normally runs that way (Assumption: wholesale runs are periodic, so Short may recur every cycle). Recommendation: Add a "business stock and fuel" group, keep it out of the daily cut, and phrase help for business users as timing ("Restock after Sun 25 Oct if you can"). Exclude vehicle finance and funeral policies from bill-move suggestions, or warn that moving them may cost fees or cover. Effort: M

BEH-06 Daily bad news risks avoidance, anxiety and exposure on shared phones

Severity: Medium Evidence: The Inbox design sends a message every morning at 07:00 that reads "Your money may run out on Fri 23 Oct... Cover: Short" (ayanda-inbox-light-390.png, src/i18n/locales/en.json:666-682). Investors look at accounts less after bad news (Karlsson, Loewenstein and Seppi 2009, the ostrich effect), so a daily Short message may lead to muting rather than action. Nudges can also have side effects: payment reminders cut late fees but raised overdraft fees for some users (Medina 2021). The message preview on a lock screen shows state and safe to spend, which on a shared or family phone can invite requests (if not Short) or shame (if Short). The privacy promise covers balance and transactions only. Recommendation: Default to messages on change of state or two days before a Short date, not daily. Offer a neutral lock-screen preview ("Your Tengo update is ready"). Lead with the next action and a specific upcoming cost, which Karlan et al. found more effective. Track opt-out rates by state in any pilot. Effort: S

BEH-07 Stokvels, burial societies, family transfers and informal credit are absent

Severity: Medium Evidence: The seed descriptions contain no stokvel, burial society, family transfer, mashonisa or airtime advance entries (src/data/seed/generate.ts). FinScope SA Consumer 2023 reports informal savings at 30 percent of adults (10 percent in 2003), burial societies at 29 percent and borrowing to buy food at 40 percent. NASASA estimates are reported as over 11 million stokvel members (Cape Argus 2024). A rotating stokvel payout is a large, infrequent deposit that can pass the 10 percent "next money" threshold and distort the income pattern (src/engine/forecast.ts:24, Assumption: payout sizes of one to several months of contributions). Contributions are only treated as bills if the amount and gap fit the monthly rule (src/engine/classify.ts:16), so weekly contributions become everyday spend. Recommendation: Add a stokvel contribution and payout, a family transfer and an informal loan repayment to at least one persona. Let users mark a deposit "one-off or stokvel payout" so it is excluded from the next money pattern, and mark a regular contribution as a commitment rather than spend. Effort: M

BEH-08 The date-first behavioural claim is unproven

Severity: Low Evidence: The pitch line "A balance tells you what you have. Tengo tells you until when" (README.md:5) implies a date changes behaviour more than a balance. I found no published experiment comparing a runway date with a plain balance. The nearest evidence is adjacent (daily framing, salience of costs, reminders). Monzo shows a "left to spend" estimate, which suggests the idea is in use but not that it works better (Monzo help page). Recommendation: Present the date-first claim as a hypothesis in the pitch and run the comparison in the user study (task T2). Effort: S

BEH-09 Account-level view can be gamed or used strategically

Severity: Low Evidence: The state depends only on the balance of the imported account (src/engine/forecast.ts, forecast). A user can move money to another account or wallet to make the state look Short, for example to show a relative, or keep income elsewhere so the app looks better than reality. Assumption: strategic display is plausible where requests from family are frequent; there is no data on how often it would occur. Recommendation: Treat this as a design input, not a flaw to police. Ask in testing how people would show or hide the screen (question F3), and keep the shared message free of amounts if users prefer. Effort: S

User-testing plan (5 to 8 participants, hackathon budget)

Aim. Check comprehension of the date, range, states and safe to spend; whether a date prompts different decisions than a balance; and how the loan check fits family obligations. This is qualitative, not a statistical test.

Participants. Six, with two optional extras if time allows. - Two e-hailing or minibus taxi drivers (Tshwane), two hairdressers or salon workers (Mamelodi), two spaza or informal traders (Tembisa). - Screener: aged 18 or over; income varies week to week; uses a smartphone with WhatsApp; has a bank account or wallet; at least three of six include someone in a stokvel and someone who regularly sends money to family. Mix of gender and age. Exclude anyone who works in banking or fintech. - Recruitment: through a community organisation, a salon or taxi rank contact, or a stokvel chairperson, with a short spoken explanation in the person's preferred language. Avoid friends of the team.

Setting. At the participant's place of work or a nearby quiet spot, outside peak trading hours, 40 to 45 minutes. Use a mid-range Android phone provided by the team with the demo installed and offline-capable, so no participant data is used and no data cost falls on them. One facilitator, one note taker who speaks the participant's language. Use the fictional personas only. Do not ask for real balances.

Consent. Plain-language verbal and written consent in the participant's language: what the study is, that the app is a demo with fictional data, that nothing they say is linked to their bank, that they can stop at any time and still receive the incentive, whether audio is recorded (ask separately), and that notes are anonymised and deleted after the hackathon. No photos of faces.

Incentive. Cash or a data or airtime voucher of about R200 to R300 per person plus transport if they travel (Assumption: a common range for short community interviews in Gauteng; confirm locally). Total for six: about R1 500 to R2 000, plus about R500 for refreshments and printing (Assumption).

Tasks and exact questions.

Warm-up (5 min) - W1. "How do you know if your money will last until the next money comes in?" - W2. "Which accounts, wallets, cash or groups does your money go through in a normal week?" - W3. "When family asks to borrow, how do you decide how much?"

T1. First look at Home (Ayanda's or Lerato's screen, 2 minutes, no help) - C1. "What does this date mean?" Success: says the money already there runs out around that day, before new money is counted. - C2. "What does Short (or Enough) mean here?" Success: links the word to next money, not to a general judgement. - C3. "The screen says likely between Thu 22 and Sun 25 Oct. What does that mean for you?" Success: understands it is uncertain, could be earlier. - C4. "What does Safe to spend today R330 mean? If you spent R330 every day this week, what would happen?" Success: says it might not be safe every day or that rent comes later. Failure: treats it as a daily allowance. - C5. "Is anything big coming after your next money?" (Lerato) Success: spots rent without opening drawers. Expected failure today, by design of BEH-01.

T2. Date versus balance (paper cards, order alternated between participants) - Card A shows only "Balance R1 877". Card B shows "Short from Fri 23 Oct, safe to spend R310". Same situation. - Q: "A friend asks you for R300 today. With this card, what would you do?" then "Would the other card change your answer? Why?" Record the amount each card leads to. Success for the hypothesis: Card B leads to a smaller or delayed amount for at least four of six, with reasons tied to the date.

T3. Loan check (Thabo's Plan screen) - Q: "Your sister needs R500 for school shoes. Use the app to decide." Observe whether they tap "Use R320". - Q: "What does Safe to lend up to R320 mean to you?" "Would you show this screen to your sister?" "If it said no amount is safe, what would you do?" - Success: understands it as a limit, not a recommendation; finds a way to give less without shame.

T4. What helps most (Ayanda) - Q: "It says spend about R250 less a day. What would you cut?" Success: does not cut stock or fuel; if they do, record as BEH-05 evidence.

T5. Message (Inbox) - Q: "If this came every morning at 7, how would you feel? Would you keep it on?" "Who else sees your phone?"

Wrap-up (5 min) - F1. "Would you trust this date? What would make you trust it more?" - F2. "What does it miss about how your money really works?" (probe cash, stokvel, family, airtime) - F3. "Would you ever want this to look better or worse than it is? When?" - F4. "On a scale of 1 to 5, how sure do you feel about your money until next week after using this?" Ask the same item before T1 to see direction only.

What counts as success or failure. - Pass to proceed: at least five of six answer C1 and C2 correctly, at least four of six answer C3 correctly, and no one describes Safe to spend as a daily allowance in C4. - Red flags that stop a pilot until fixed: two or more treat safe to spend or safe to lend as permission; two or more cut stock or fuel in T4; two or more say they would turn messages off in T5 because they feel judged. - Record cash, stokvel, family and wallet flows in W2 for each participant to size BEH-02 and BEH-07.

Running it on a hackathon budget. Two team members, one afternoon per area across two days, six sessions. Paper cards and the offline demo, no recording equipment beyond a phone if consent is given. Write up a one-page table of answers per question and three quotes per finding within 24 hours. Total cost about R2 000 to R2 500 plus transport (Assumption).

Top three risks

  1. Harmful action on a "safe" figure. People treat safe to spend or safe to lend as permission and drop a level or miss rent, while the app said "safe" (BEH-01, BEH-03).
  2. False precision from a partial view. Cash, wallets, stokvels and family transfers make the single-account date wrong in ways the user cannot see, which damages trust after the first miss (BEH-02, BEH-07).
  3. Disengagement of the people who need it most. Daily Short messages and advice that cuts business inputs push the most stretched users to mute or abandon the app (BEH-05, BEH-06).

Score

5 out of 10. The question, daily framing and transparency are strong, but the "safe" frames can mislead, the single-account view misses how the personas actually handle money, and the core behavioural claim is untested.
