4.13Hackathon judge panel

Reviewer file: docs/review/judges.md. Findings are listed in full in Appendix A.

Simulated judging panel. We are not FNB judges, have no access to FNB's internal rubric or weighting, and score only from the code, the shared preparation in docs/review/prep/, the screenshots, and the twelve other reviewer files, which this panel was told to read.

Criteria used. The brief names seven criteria: Product, Creativity, Usability, Sustainability, Impact, Presentation and Intelligence. The public FNB App of the Year FAQ (https://appoftheyear.co.za/faqs/, accessed 2026-10-20) defines the same seven for the App of the Year awards and says round 1 judges "play around" with apps loaded on devices. I did not find a hackathon-specific scoring sheet for 2026, or anything public about the "Financial inclusion and money confidence" track, so I applied the FAQ wording to this entry as follows:

Criterion Public FAQ wording (summarised) How this panel applied it
Product Complete, polished, works as a user would expect, solves the stated need, market need, traction Does the build work end to end and answer "is my money enough until the next money" correctly and consistently? Traction counts as zero today.
Creativity New application or service, creative approach, new approach to an old problem Is the date-first, irregular-income framing new compared with what South African bank apps publicly offer?
Usability Easy to use, clear, simple, intuitive, obvious what to do next First two minutes on a phone for each persona, plus accessibility and offline evidence.
Sustainability Potential of app and team, next level, path to revenue Business model, data route, defensibility, readiness for a real backend.
Impact Size of problem, indispensability, importance Size and fit of the segment, minus the risk of harm and the parts of the segment the product cannot see.
Presentation How well the team communicates the idea and its relevance to society What a judge sees without the founder: landing page, in-app story, README, and honesty of claims. No pitch deck or demo script was in the repository, so this is scored on the product's own story.
Intelligence Can the app forecast and predict likely future behaviour by analysing patterns in data Quality of the forecast engine, its validation, and how it behaves when the patterns break.

Verdict

Tengo is a well-made, honest prototype with one clear and good idea: tell an irregular earner the date their money runs out and whether it reaches the next money, not just a balance. It works end to end, it is fast and accessible by tool measures, and its engine is pure, tested and explainable, which maps directly onto the Intelligence criterion. It would lose points in judging for three reasons that are visible within a minute of use: the Home screen contradicts itself (Short beside "Safe to spend R310" and a green "Enough" on the chart, "Enough until" beside a Tight chip, the latter also on the landing hero); the landing page claims 11 official languages when all 10 non-English files are English; and some outputs could cause harm (it tells Thabo to pay his car instalment late). Most of these are copy and rule fixes measured in hours. Sustainability and Impact are capped by things a hackathon cannot fully fix (no users, no data route, a thin single-bank revenue case), but an honest business page and a handful of user sessions would move both. On the evidence today we would shortlist it in round 1 and expect it to struggle in a final unless the Home contradictions and the language claim are fixed first.

What works

  • A sharp, explainable core answer. The date is the largest element on Home at every width, with state shown by word, icon, colour and bar count (ayanda-home-dark-1280.png, thabo-home-light-390.png; UX "What works"; src/components/StateChip.tsx). Product lead and commercial reviewers each found no verified South African bank feature that projects a run-out date against irregular income cadence (PROD verdict, COMM "What works").
  • Intelligence that is real, not decorative. The engine detects weekday and interval income rhythms, recurring bills, and rolling spend percentiles (src/engine/forecast.ts, classify.ts:16). On the backtest, with 90 days of history, mean absolute error is 2.1 days against a naive baseline of 3.4, with near-zero bias and 71.8 percent band coverage (docs/review/backtest/results/summary_headline.csv, data scientist "What works"). The engine has 97.6 percent statement coverage and 66 passing unit tests (docs/review/prep/coverage.txt, checks.md).
  • Polish and accessibility above hackathon norm. Lighthouse mobile landing 94 performance and 100 accessibility; 221 Playwright tests pass; axe finds 1 violation over 15 routes in both themes (fact sheet; docs/review/prep/playwright.txt; A11Y "What works"). Reduced motion, reflow at 320 px and target sizes all pass (A11Y "What works").
  • Honest demo labelling. "Demo | Fictional Data" appears on every app screen and simulated privacy and messaging are labelled (landing-dark-1280.png footer; LEGAL "What works"; UX "What works").
  • Features that fit the segment. The family loan check and daily safe-to-spend unit match how informal earners decide (BEH "What works", citing Hershfield, Shu and Benartzi 2020). The privacy centre demonstrates consent, export, deletion and human review in working screens (SEC "What works").
  • Built to be embedded. TengoRepository and MessageWriter give a bank the seams to supply data and channel (PROD, COMM, TECH "What works").

Criterion scores

Criterion Score (0 to 10) Justification and evidence Biggest gap Single change that would raise it most
Product 6 Complete and working across 10 screens, both themes and three widths (fact sheet "Screens", 221 Playwright passes). Answers its one question for all three personas. Loses points because the main screen gives conflicting answers (UX-01, UX-02, UX-03, PROD-03, INV-03), data never ages after import so the state drifts to Enough (TECH-01), and there is no traction or real data (INV-01, DATA-01). The headline, safe to spend figure and chart disagree on the screen judges see first. Make one rule drive Home: state-specific hero label, a daily limit tied to the state, no state word on the chart readout (JUDGE-01).
Creativity 7 Date-first framing for people without a payday, an inferred "next money" window, three plain states and a loan-to-family check are not publicly offered by the SA banks reviewed (PROD verdict, COMM competitor table). Not a 9 because the arithmetic is simple and "safe to spend until payday" exists in other apps (INV-02, COMM-01), and the date-beats-balance claim is untested (BEH-08). The idea's novelty rests on the framing; the cash and stokvel reality that would make it uniquely South African is missing (BEH-02, BEH-07). A one-tap "cash in hand" entry that feeds the forecast (JUDGE-08).
Usability 6 Strong base: clean hierarchy, designed empty and error states, keyboard and drawer patterns, Nielsen average 3.6 of 5 (UX heuristic table). Pulled down by the lowest heuristic, consistency of numbers (2 of 5), about 20 figures on Ayanda's first screen (A11Y-05), three different next-money dates (UX-04), a pre-filled Plan that shows a loss before the user acts (UX-06), and focus hidden under the phone tab bar (A11Y-04). Offline: the app's own test passes an offline reload (tests/flows.spec.ts:131) and the accessibility reviewer found a blank page offline after one visit (A11Y-02). Both are true: offline works only from the second load (TECH-05). A stressed user cannot tell which number to believe. Same as Product: reconcile Home (JUDGE-01), then start Plan empty (JUDGE-10).
Sustainability 4 Right shape for a bank licence (COMM "What works", PROD "What works") and cheap to run if messages go in-app (COMM-03). But no business model in the repo (COMM-11, INV-07), no data route beyond CSV (PROD-06, INV-04), no moat against the bank building it (COMM-01, INV-02), and single-bank economics short of break-even at the base fee (COMM-02: about 44 000 MAU against about 82 000 needed at R5, all marked Assumption in model-inputs.md). The repository contract is not ready for a real backend (TECH-03). No stated buyer, price or data route. A one-page business case: bank-embedded licence, pilot KPIs, data contract, honest moat (JUDGE-06).
Impact 5 The problem is large: 3.6 million in the informal sector and only 18.3 percent of the poorest 40 percent can easily raise emergency money (COMM "What works", Stats SA and World Bank sources). But the product sees one bank account while the segment lives in cash and stokvels (BEH-02, COMM-04, INV-10), the engine shows Enough when income has stopped (RISK-01, RISK-03), lumpy earners shown Enough ran short 11.6 percent of the time on synthetic data (DATA-02), some advice could harm (LEGAL-01, RISK-09), and the multilingual reach is not real yet (L10N-01). No user has tested it (INV-01). Possible harm and no evidence of benefit. Remove harmful suggestions (JUDGE-03), then run 5 to 8 user sessions and quote them (JUDGE-07).
Presentation 5 The tagline "A balance tells you what you have. Tengo tells you until when" is the strongest line in the entry (COMM, INV "What works"), and the landing page explains three steps clearly (landing-dark-1280.png). But the landing hero itself shows "Enough until Tue 27 Oct" beside "Tight" (UX-03), the stats row claims "11 Official languages" (COMM-08, INV-05, L10N-01) and "0 Bank logins needed", which conflicts with the bank-embed story (COMM-07). Privacy screens show green ticks beside controls that do not exist (SEC-02). No pitch deck, demo script or user quotes are in the repository. The first screen a judge sees contains a contradiction and an untrue claim. Fix the landing hero label and replace the language stat with an honest one (JUDGE-02), then rehearse a demo built on the fixed screens (JUDGE-12).
Intelligence 6 This is the criterion the engine was built for: it analyses patterns to forecast income timing and run-out, and the backtest shows it beats a naive baseline with 60 or more days (DATA "What works"). Held back because validation is circular (DATA-01: Ayanda's shipped history is one path), the band is too narrow near run-out (DATA-03: 53.5 percent coverage within 6 days), short imports are worse than the baseline (DATA-04), and pattern breaks are not detected: stopped income, payday morning, windfalls (RISK-01, RISK-03, RISK-05, RISK-10, DATA-05). The engine assumes the pattern holds when the point of the product is that it often does not. Add pattern-break guards (missed paydays, no income, payday today, stale data) with tests, and show the backtest honestly (JUDGE-04, JUDGE-05).

Total: 39 of 70. Average: 5.6 of 10.

The spread is deliberate. Creativity is highest because the framing is distinct. Sustainability is lowest because nothing in the repository answers who pays or where real data comes from. Reviewer scores ranged from 4 (credit risk, localisation, commercial, investor) to 6 (technology, UX, accessibility), which matches this panel's split between a strong build and a weak case.

What to improve

Ranked by expected judging score gain per hour of work before submission. Effort is for the change itself, not for every underlying finding.

JUDGE-01 Make one rule drive the Home screen
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Ayanda: hero "Short from Fri 23 Oct", card "Safe to spend today R310", chart readout "Enough" (ayanda-home-dark-1280.png, seen by this panel). Thabo: "Enough until" above a "Tight" chip (thabo-home-light-390.png, landing-dark-1280.png). See UX-01, UX-02, UX-03, UX-04, PROD-03, INV-03, BEH-04, LEGAL-08, RISK-04, A11Y-03. Seven reviewers raised it independently. The credit risk reviewer notes R310 is in fact a conservative ceiling (following it leaves R17 on 26 Oct, RISK "What works"), so the fix is mainly the label and placement, not the maths.
  • Recommendation: State-specific hero label ("Lasts until (on a normal week)" for Tight). For Short, show "To reach Mon 26 Oct, keep to R310 a day. You usually spend R620." Drop the state word from today's chart readout or say "Enough if money lands as usual" in the readout and in aria-valuetext. Show one next-money date. Re-capture the landing mock.
  • Effort: S (about half a day). Raises Product, Usability and Presentation.
JUDGE-02 Replace the 11-languages claim and fix the page language
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Landing stat "11 Official languages" (landing-dark-1280.png); all 10 non-English files are byte-identical to English (L10N-01); html lang says Tshivenda over English text (A11Y-01, L10N-05); the translation script cannot produce Tshivenda (L10N-02). See COMM-08, INV-05. A judge who picks isiZulu sees English and discounts the rest.
  • Recommendation: Change the stat to "Built for 11 languages" or "English now, more coming". Set lang="en" while a locale is pending. If time allows, have a first-language speaker write the money keys (state, hero, safe to spend, messages) for one Gauteng language and mark it reviewed.
  • Effort: S for copy and lang (under an hour); M for one reviewed language. Raises Presentation and Impact.
JUDGE-03 Remove the outputs that could cause harm
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: "Pay Car instalment (R3 450) after Tue 27 Oct instead of before" (thabo-home-light-390.png; src/engine/help.ts:62 sorts by amount only). "Safe to lend up to" with a one-tap "Use" button; Lerato told she can spend R330, set aside R280 and lend R280 from the same buffer. The only "not advice" line is at the foot of a drawer. See LEGAL-01, LEGAL-02, RISK-09, RISK-11, BEH-01, BEH-03, BEH-05, PROD-04, INV-09.
  • Recommendation: Never suggest moving credit, insurance, funeral or rent payments; add a unit test. Rename "Safe to lend up to" to "You could lend up to" with "and stay Tight". Exclude stock and fuel from the daily cut. Put "An estimate from your past money, not advice" under the hero, Plan and help card.
  • Effort: S to M (about a day). Raises Impact and Product, and removes the question a judge is most likely to end on.
JUDGE-04 Teach the engine to notice when the pattern breaks
  • Severity: High
  • Evidence: Income stopped 35 days ago still gives Enough with high confidence and R1 990 safe to lend (RISK-01, S11). No income at all can show Enough (RISK-03; forecast.test.ts:116 asserts it). Payday morning shows Short (RISK-10). Imported data never ages, so Ayanda drifts from Short to Enough after 30 days (TECH-01). A missed payout raises false reassurance from 7.6 to 36.8 percent (DATA-05).
  • Recommendation: Add four guarded rules with tests: missed paydays mark the pattern "paused" (state at most Tight, lend R0); no pattern shows "No regular income found"; "Payday today, not in yet" instead of Short; store an asOf date and show "Your data is N days old". Show one of these in the demo; a judge scoring Intelligence will value an engine that knows when not to trust itself.
  • Effort: S each, about a day together. Raises Intelligence and Impact.
JUDGE-05 Put the backtest in the pitch, with its limits
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: MAE 2.1 days against a naive 3.4, band coverage 71.8 percent, Enough reliable for regular earners (DATA "What works", docs/review/backtest/results/summary_headline.csv, charts/calibration.png). But all of it is synthetic and partly circular (DATA-01), and Tight was followed by running short 24.4 percent of the time (DATA-02). No competitor publishes accuracy for irregular income (COMM-01).
  • Recommendation: One slide or About section: "On synthetic data: within about 2 days, beats a simple baseline, worst for lumpy earners. Not yet tested on real statements." Rename "Likely between" to state its coverage. Optionally tighten Tight copy to say running short is possible.
  • Effort: S (two hours). Raises Intelligence and Presentation.
JUDGE-06 Write the one-page business case and pick one distribution story
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: No buyer, pricing or metrics in README or BRIEF (COMM-11, INV-07). Landing claims "0 Bank logins needed" while the only realistic route is inside a bank app (COMM-07, PROD-06, INV-04). Overlap with nav» Money and Standard Bank Future Payments is unaddressed (PROD-02). Single-bank economics need a platform floor (COMM-02); daily paid WhatsApp costs more than the likely fee (COMM-03).
  • Recommendation: Add a page: B2B2C licence embedded in a bank app, pilot fee plus per-active-user fee plus floor, in-app push by default, a "bank already has versus Tengo adds" table, a data contract (balance, facility limit, debit orders, 60 days of transactions; PROD-01), and four pilot KPIs. State plainly that the moat is tested copy, accuracy evidence and segment design, not code.
  • Effort: S (half a day). Raises Sustainability by the most of any single change.
JUDGE-07 Run five to eight user sessions and quote them
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: No users, interviews or sessions anywhere in the repo (INV-01, COMM-05). The date-first claim is a hypothesis (BEH-08). The behavioural reviewer wrote a ready plan with tasks, pass criteria and an assumed budget of about R2 000 to R2 500 (BEH user-testing plan).
  • Recommendation: Run the BEH plan's T1 and T2 with six drivers, hairdressers or traders after JUDGE-01 and JUDGE-03 land. Report how many read the date correctly and quote three people.
  • Effort: M (two days). Lower score per hour than the fixes above, but the only change that moves Impact and Product traction with evidence.
JUDGE-08 Add cash in hand
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: The engine sees one bank account; FinScope 2023 reports 94 percent withdraw cash monthly (BEH-02); two of three personas earn cash (COMM-04, INV-10); stokvels and family transfers are absent (BEH-07). The product lead would pilot gig earners paid into the account first (PROD-07).
  • Recommendation: A "Cash in hand" figure updated in one tap and a quick money in or out entry that feeds the forecast, with "Based on your bank account only" under the date until it is used.
  • Effort: M (one to two days). Raises Creativity and Impact; it is the clearest answer to "why not just a bank feature".
JUDGE-09 Make offline work on the first visit
  • Severity: Medium
  • Evidence: The service worker precaches only /, the manifest and icons, and registers on load, so first-visit JS and CSS are not cached (A11Y-02, TECH-05). The repo test reloads online before going offline (tests/flows.spec.ts:131-140), so it passes while a first-time user offline gets a blank page. Both reviewers are right; they tested different sequences.
  • Recommendation: Precache the build output at install (vite-plugin-pwa or a generated manifest), add a plain offline fallback, and change the test to go offline straight after the first visit. Then the loadshedding claim holds in a live demo.
  • Effort: S (two to three hours). Raises Usability and Impact.
JUDGE-10 Small first-impression fixes on Plan, consent and privacy
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: Plan opens with a R500 what-if and R300 loan already applied (UX-06). Daily messages default on at consent (UX-09, LEGAL-03, SEC-06). Privacy shows green ticks beside encryption and access claims and a retention rule that never runs (SEC-01, SEC-02). Tight and Short are almost the same colour in light theme (UX-05).
  • Recommendation: Start Plan empty, default messages off, swap ticks for a neutral "planned" icon and write future controls as "would", shift light Tight to amber.
  • Effort: S (two hours together). Raises Usability and Presentation, and avoids a judge reading simulated controls as real claims.
JUDGE-11 Confirm the name and clear the brand basics
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: The Tshivenda meaning of the name is unconfirmed and may mean "price" rather than "value" (L10N-12, INV-11); the heading font needs a commercial licence and the logo is a placeholder (COMM-10).
  • Recommendation: Confirm the name with two Tshivenda speakers before telling the name story on stage.
  • Effort: S. Raises Presentation.
JUDGE-12 Script the demo around the fixed screens
  • Severity: Low
  • Evidence: No demo script, deck or video is in the repository. The strongest moments are Ayanda's Short warning paired with an action, the Why drawer and the loan check (UX walkthrough, BEH "What works"); the weakest are the language picker and the help card (walkthrough items 5 and 10).
  • Recommendation: Three minutes: tagline, Ayanda Short with the reconciled daily limit, Why this date, loan check on Thabo, one pattern-break case (JUDGE-04), backtest slide with its caveat, business page. Avoid the language picker unless JUDGE-02 shipped a reviewed language.
  • Effort: S. Raises Presentation.

Top three risks

  1. A judge finds the contradiction first. The landing hero and Ayanda's Home disagree with themselves; a judge who notices will doubt the forecast even where it is right (JUDGE-01; UX-01 to UX-03, INV-03).
  2. A false claim costs credibility across all criteria. "11 Official languages" and green privacy ticks are checkable in seconds and are not true (JUDGE-02, JUDGE-10; L10N-01, SEC-02).
  3. The judge ends on harm. "Pay your car instalment late" or "Safe to lend" to someone already Tight turns an inclusion pitch into a liability question (JUDGE-03; LEGAL-01, RISK-09, RISK-11).

Score

6 out of 10 (criterion average 5.6). A distinct, well-built and honest prototype whose main screen contradicts itself and whose case for revenue, reach and real-world accuracy is not yet made; the top five fixes are about three working days and would move it to roughly 7.

Sources

  • FNB App of the Year, FAQs (judging rounds and the seven criteria): https://appoftheyear.co.za/faqs/ (accessed 2026-10-20)
  • FNB App of the Year, 2025 Hackathon programme (found in search; listed as the closest hackathon document, covers schedule rather than scoring, not used for criteria): https://appoftheyear.co.za/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/2025-Hackathon-programme.pdf (accessed 2026-10-20)

5Forecast backtest

The data scientist backtested the engine on held-out days generated by the app's own seed generator (method and code in docs/review/backtest/). The credit risk analyst separately stress-tested edge cases the generator does not produce. The synthetic data and the engine share assumptions, so these results show internal consistency and relative behaviour, not real-world accuracy (DATA-01).

5.1 Headline results

SliceCasesMAE daysNaive MAEWithin 3 daysBand coverageState accuracyFalse reassurance
shipped generator, 60 day history, all balances4321.943.3676.1%65.7%70.6%7.6%
other seeds, 90 day history, all balances21602.073.3778.1%71.8%71.9%7.6%
other seeds, 60 day history, all balances21602.223.2978.2%71.2%71.3%8.4%
shipped, 60 day, engine-independent balances only2162.033.8475.8%70.2%91.7%0.5%
shipped, 60 day, startingBalance targets only2161.862.8876.4%61.1%49.5%14.8%

Source: docs/review/backtest/results/summary_headline.csv. MAE is mean absolute error in days between the forecast runway date and the actual date computed the same way on held-out data. Band coverage is how often the actual date fell inside the stated range. State accuracy compares the state shown with hindsight using the data scientist's 20 percent buffer rule.

5.2 How to rerun

Backtest of src/engine/forecast.ts against synthetic histories from src/data/seed/generate.ts. Findings and definitions are in docs/review/data-scientist.md.

Read this first. The generator and the engine were written by the same author with matching assumptions (weekly or interval income, monthly bills on a fixed day, stationary everyday spend). Good numbers here show the engine is internally consistent with the world it was designed for. They are weak evidence of accuracy on real bank statements.

How to rerun

From the repository root:

```sh

1. Backtest (about 1 minute). Writes CSV files to docs/review/backtest/results/.

npx vite-node docs/review/backtest/backtest.ts

2. Bootstrap intervals for the headline metrics (standard library only).

python3 -I docs/review/backtest/bootstrap.py

3. Charts. Needs matplotlib in any Python 3 environment.

python3 -m venv /tmp/tengo-bt && /tmp/tengo-bt/bin/python -m pip install matplotlib /tmp/tengo-bt/bin/python -I docs/review/backtest/charts.py ```

Everything is deterministic: same code, same results. The script imports the real engine and generator and changes nothing in src/.

Design in one paragraph

3 personas x 24 cut-off dates (every 15 days from 2025-10-06, so they cover 12 months and rotate through all weekdays). Two data sources: shipped is the app's own generateHistory(persona, cutoff + 30) split at the cut-off (60 days history, 30 days hold-out); seeds is a verified line-for-line copy of the generator's persona rules run with 5 other seeds per cut-off (120 independent paths per persona, 90 days history, 30 days hold-out). The script checks that the copy reproduces generateHistory exactly before running. History is truncated to 14, 28, 60 and 90 days and reclassified on the history alone. Six balances per case: three from the app's startingBalance (enough, tight, short targets) and three engine-independent ones (7, 14, 21 days of mean daily outflow). Perturbations (shipped, 60 days) change only the hold-out.

Files

File What it holds
backtest.ts The backtest.
bootstrap.py 95 percent bootstrap intervals, resampling independent paths.
charts.py Charts from the CSV results.
results/cases.csv One row per case (12 528 rows), every input and outcome.
results/summary_headline.csv Headline slices.
results/summary_by_persona*.csv, summary_by_history.csv, summary_by_persona_history*.csv Breakdowns.
results/summary_by_lead_time.csv By how many days until money actually ran out.
results/summary_by_scenario*.csv By balance scenario.
results/perturbation*.csv Shocks the generator never produces.
results/state_confusion.csv Shown state against hindsight state.
results/calibration_points.csv Signed errors and band position per observed case.
results/generator_diversity.csv Distinct random paths the shipped generator produces across cut-offs.
results/bootstrap_ci.csv Confidence intervals.
results/console.txt Console output of the last run.
charts/*.png Error distribution, calibration, error by history, state confusion, perturbation, band position.

Column meanings in cases.csv

Day indices count from the cut-off (day 0 is the cut-off day, the engine's "today"). 180 means beyond the engine horizon.

  • fcCentral, fcBandFrom, fcBandTo: engine central zero date and band (heavy path to light path).
  • actualRunway: first day the balance minus actual outflows (spend and bills, no income) is zero or below; -1 if not within 30 days.
  • err: fcCentral - actualRunway. Positive means the forecast was too late (too optimistic).
  • baselineErr: same for the naive baseline, balance divided by the mean daily outflow of the last 28 days.
  • bandCovered: 1 inside the band, 0 outside, empty when undecidable (actual not within 30 days and band reaches past day 29).
  • ranOutWithIncome: first day the real balance, including real income after day 0, is zero or below.
  • nmActual: first day from day 1 with income at least the engine's significance threshold (10 percent of typical monthly income from the history).
  • hindState: Short if the outflow-only balance reached zero before nmActual; Enough if at least 20 percent of the outflows of that period was left; Tight otherwise. hindState10 and hindState30 use 10 and 30 percent.
  • realShort: 1 when the real balance with all real income hit zero before the next money.

5.4 What synthetic data does and does not prove

It shows the engine behaves as designed, is stable across seeds and weekdays, degrades predictably with short histories and beats a naive baseline when its own assumptions hold. It does not show that real irregular earners spend, earn or pay bills the way the generator assumes; that the range band is calibrated on real data; or that the states would be right for real people. That needs consented real statements, a held-out period and the same metrics, before any accuracy claim is made in the pitch.

6Financial model

The model is docs/review/model.xlsx: an Inputs sheet with 120 inputs (each with low, base and high values and a source URL with access date or the label Assumption), a Model sheet computing every line for three scenarios side by side, a 24 month Projection, Sensitivity tables and Metrics. Conservative takes the unfavourable end of every range, Ambitious the favourable end. All 1 474 formulas evaluate without errors. Of the 116 inputs prepared by the commercial reviewer, 69 are sourced, 46 are Assumptions and one (FNB's unpaid debit order fee) could not be sourced. Bank outcomes are modelled possibilities, not facts.

6.1 Market sizing

Stats SA's QLFS for Q2 2026 counts 3.6 million people in informal sector employment and 1.1 million in private households. The total market of adults with irregular or informal income is an Assumption of 6.0 million (range 4.7 to 9.0 million). With an account and a smartphone, the serviceable market is 3 300 000 in the base case. One FNB-sized partner (8.16 million digitally active customers, BusinessDay, 16 September 2026) yields about 44 064 monthly active users at steady state in the base case (1 867 conservative, 330 480 ambitious).

6.2 Unit economics and business models

OutputConservativeBaseAmbitious
Variable cost per MAU per month, embeddedR6.84R1.19R0.36
Fixed cost per monthR600 430R310 403R180 393
(a) Bank licence: contribution per MAU per month-R4.84R3.81R11.64
(a) MAU needed to break evennever81 37415 496
(b) Revenue share: contribution per MAU per month-R6.84-R0.68R5.57
(c) Consumer subscription: contribution per MAU per month-R30.53-R2.37R1.15
(c) Consumer: lifetime value to acquisition cost-0.31x-0.16x0.36x
(d) Sponsored: contribution per user per month-R12.07R1.89R11.58

Source: model.xlsx, Scenarios sheet. Blank break-even means contribution is zero or negative, so the model never breaks even.

Which model fits. (a) a per-active-user licence to a bank, with a pilot fee and an annual platform minimum, is the only model with positive contribution in the base case and a credible distribution path. (b) revenue share depends on a bank value case that is thin and unproven. (c) a consumer subscription loses money in every scenario because conversion is low and acquisition is expensive for this segment. (d) sponsorship is a useful secondary channel for reach outside banks but does not fund a company on its own. This matches the commercial reviewer (COMM verdict).

6.3 Value to a bank

Base value to the bank is R30.50 per active user per year, against a base licence of R60 per user per year: about 0.51 rand of value per rand of licence. The case rests on retention, balances and avoided calls, all Assumptions. Avoided unpaid debit orders also remove fee income from the bank, which offsets part of the operational saving. A pilot with a control group is the only way to establish this.

6.4 Break-even, scenarios and sensitivity

Model (a), one partner bankConservativeBaseAmbitious
First profitable monthbeyond 24beyond 241
Peak funding needR13 598 817R4 728 272R0
Cumulative cash at month 24-R13 598 817-R4 728 272R59 734 868

The licence fee per user dominates: at R2 a month break-even needs about 381 000 active users, at R12 about 29 000. Support cost per user and the team cost come next. Messaging and LLM costs barely matter when notifications go in-app; paid daily WhatsApp would cost more than the base licence leaves (COMM-03).

7Impact case and measurement plan

Impact must separate what Tengo produces (outputs) from what changes in people's lives (outcomes). No outcome has been measured.

TypeMetricBase case valueHow to measure
OutputPeople reached per year (one bank plus a sponsor programme)54 064Activated users and sponsor sign-ups
OutputForecasts viewed, messages opted innot measuredConsented product analytics
OutcomeDays of cover gained per year (all users)211 507Forecast date against actual run-out, before and after
OutcomeShort episodes avoided per yearAssumption: 0.5 per userBalance hitting zero before next money, pilot against control
OutcomeFees avoided by users per yearR1 321 920Unpaid debit order and late fees, pilot against control
OutcomeConfidence that money lastsAssumption: +5 pointsSurvey item below, before and after, with a comparison group

Proposed survey item. "How sure are you that your money will last until your next money?" on a five point scale from "not at all" to "completely". The baseline proxy from the World Bank Findex 2025 is that 30.8 percent of South African adults could easily raise emergency money in 30 days, 18.3 percent among the poorest 40 percent (model-inputs.md).

7.1 Measurement plan

  1. Before any pilot: the six-person comprehension test (behavioural chapter) to check people read the date, range and states as intended.
  2. Pilot design: a randomised or matched control group at the partner bank, 3 to 6 months, with outcomes defined in advance.
  3. Track the product metrics in model.xlsx (Metrics sheet): activation, weekly use, day 7 and day 30 retention, imports completed, messages opted in, field forecast accuracy and state transitions acted on.
  4. Report harms as well as benefits: users who followed "safe to spend" and ran short, anxiety reported with Short, any late payment linked to a suggestion.

8Cross-cutting themes and conflicts

8.1 Themes raised by several reviewers

  1. Home contradicts itself. Raised by product, UX, accessibility, credit risk, behavioural, legal, investor and the judges (PROD-03, UX-01 to UX-04, A11Y-03, RISK-04, BEH-04, LEGAL-08, INV-03). The most cited problem and the cheapest to fix.
  2. The engine misses pattern breaks. Stopped or absent income, payday morning, stale imported data, bills just after payday (RISK-01 to RISK-03, RISK-10, TECH-01, DATA-02).
  3. Advice-like or harmful outputs. Late-payment suggestions, "safe" wording, lending framed as safe (LEGAL-01, LEGAL-02, RISK-09, RISK-11, BEH-03).
  4. Claims that are not yet true. Eleven languages, retention, data minimisation, security ticks (INV-05, L10N-01, SEC-01 to SEC-03).
  5. The money people live on is invisible. Cash, wallets, stokvels and family transfers (BEH-02, BEH-07, COMM-04, INV-10, PROD-01).
  6. No evidence yet. No users, synthetic validation, no bank value proof (INV-01, DATA-01, COMM-05).

8.2 Conflicts and how they were resolved

ConflictResolution
Offline: the accessibility reviewer reports a blank page offline after a first visit (A11Y-02); the app's own Playwright test passes an offline reload. Both are true. A synthesis test (docs/review/tools/synthesis/offline-matrix.csv) shows offline works only after a second load, because the service worker caches files fetched after it takes control. The app's test reloads before going offline, which hides the gap. A11Y-02 stands.
Novelty: the product lead and commercial lead find no South African bank offering a date-first view for irregular income; the investor says FNB has shown funds after debit orders since 2018. Compatible. FNB's feature (BusinessTech, 11 June 2018, cited by the investor) shows money after known payments, not a run-out date with inferred next money. The insight is distinct in framing and segment but not defensible as technology (COMM-01, INV-02).
Severity of legal risk: the legal advisor sees low risk for the demo; the credit risk analyst rates the same outputs as High harm. Both stand: they answer different questions. As a demo with fictional data the legal exposure is low; for real users the harm risk is high. The roadmap treats the fixes as before-submission items because judges will read them as product judgement.
Translation route: the build and earlier guidance assumed Google Cloud Translation covers all 11 languages; the localisation reviewer says it does not support Tshivenda. Verified on Google's supported-languages page (last updated 2026-10-13, accessed 2026-10-20): Tshivenda is absent; the other nine non-English languages are supported by the standard NMT model. Tshivenda needs a human translator (L10N-02).
Stopped income: the credit risk analyst reports Enough after 35 days without pay. Confirmed in the analyst's saved run (tools/credit-risk/results.md, case S11: Enough with R1 990 set aside after five missed paydays).
Scores: reviewers range from 4 to 6, with technical roles higher and case-making roles lower. Not reconciled into one number on purpose. The spread reflects a strong build and a weak case, which is the main message of this report.

Highest ratings

Six risks are rated 20 or more: R1 (contradictory Home) and R12 (no backend security for real data) at 25, then R2 and R3 (safe figures and false reassurance), R8 (no evidence) and R15 (invisible cash) at 20. R1 is the only one of these that can be fixed in hours before submission. The others decide whether a pilot is safe.

Defensibility: design partnerships for irregular earners, evidence from pilots, second bank or sponsor channel COMM-01, INV-02, PROD-02, COMM-09 L

Engine performance work (worker thread, memoisation) and removal of the demo latency TECH-09, TECH-10, TECH-11 S
Strategic use and gaming of the account view; anxiety and avoidance research BEH-09, BEH-06 M

11Judge Q&A and demo script

Prepared 2026-10-20 from the 13 reviewer files. Answers are what the founder can honestly say today, based on what exists: a working demo with synthetic data, no users, no bank data, no backend. Finding ids point to the evidence in findings.csv. Where an answer depends on a fix from ROADMAP.md, it says so.

Twenty likely questions

1. How many real irregular earners have used this? None yet. It is a demo built on three fictional composites. Our next step is a six-person comprehension test with drivers, hairdressers and spaza owners (BEH user-testing plan; INV-01).

2. How accurate is the date? On real data, unknown. On synthetic data the forecast date is off by 2.1 days on average with 90 days of history, against 3.4 days for a naive baseline, and the actual date falls inside our range 72 percent of the time. The test data comes from the same assumptions as the engine, so this is a consistency check, not proof (DATA-01, backtest/results/summary_headline.csv).

3. Banks already show money left after debit orders. What is new? Those tools assume a payday. Tengo infers when the next money usually lands from irregular deposits, shows a range rather than a single figure, and turns it into Enough, Tight or Short in plain words. The arithmetic is simple; the design for people without a payday is the contribution (INV-02, PROD-02, COMM-01).

4. Why would a bank not just build it? It could. We pitch Tengo as a tested, explainable layer a bank can switch on for irregular earners quickly, licensed per active user, with the rules, copy and explanations already worked out. The moat is evidence and design for this segment, not code (COMM-01).

5. Where does the data come from? Today, a CSV statement read in the browser. In a pilot, the host bank's own transaction data. We do not rely on open finance, which South Africa has not adopted at scale (PROD-06, INV-04).

6. Who pays? A bank, per active user, with a pilot fee and an annual minimum. Users in this segment should not pay. Our base model needs about 81 000 active users at R5 a month to break even, so one bank alone is not enough at base adoption; a second bank or a sponsor programme closes the gap (COMM-02, model.xlsx Scenarios).

7. Ayanda is Short but told R310 is safe to spend, and the chart says Enough. Which is it? If fixed (roadmap item 2): "We fixed that. One rule now drives the screen: when Short, the daily budget is marked not advised, and the chart is labelled as what happens if money lands as usual." If not fixed: "That is a real inconsistency. The three figures answer different questions, and we are merging them so the screen says one thing" (UX-01, UX-02, RISK-04, INV-03).

8. Is "safe to spend" or "safe to lend" financial advice? We present factual estimates, not recommendations, and the reviewers' view is that most of this is likely outside the FAIS meaning of advice. The wording "safe" is too strong, so we are renaming it to describe consequences, and the disclaimer moves next to the figures. Inside a bank it would go through the bank's compliance review. This is not a legal opinion (LEGAL-02, LEGAL-05).

9. Could it tell someone to do something harmful? In the demo, "What helps most" could suggest paying a car instalment later. We are removing bill-move suggestions for credit, insurance and rent, and never suggesting borrowing or cutting business stock (LEGAL-01, RISK-09, roadmap item 7).

10. Your users run on cash and stokvels. How does a bank statement see that? It does not, today. Cash in hand and quick manual entries are the next feature; until then the screen should say it covers this account only (BEH-02, COMM-04, INV-10).

11. What happens if someone's income stops? In the demo the engine still expects it, which is wrong. The fix treats overdue income as late and lowers confidence, so a stopped income cannot show Enough (RISK-01, RISK-03, roadmap item 8).

12. You claim 11 languages. Can I see isiZulu? Not yet. All 11 are wired in, but only English is written. Google's translation API does not support Tshivenda, so that language needs a human translator. Once roadmap item 1 is done, the landing page says "built for 11, English today"; until then, say it out loud before a judge finds it (L10N-01, L10N-02, INV-05).

13. Where is the data stored and who can see it? In this demo, only in the browser on the device. Nothing is sent anywhere. A real service would need an authenticated backend, encryption and a data protection impact assessment before any real data is used (SEC-04).

14. Do your privacy controls meet POPIA? No claim is made. They are labelled "Simulated privacy controls. Not a compliance certification." Some screens describe rules the demo does not enforce, such as 12 month retention; we are rewording those as demo statements (SEC-01, SEC-02).

15. Does it work offline during loadshedding? Yes from the second visit, because the app caches itself after it loads. A first visit followed by going offline shows a blank page today; precaching at install fixes that (A11Y-02, roadmap item 9).

16. Is it accessible? Automated WCAG 2.2 checks find almost nothing across 15 pages in both themes, it reflows at 320 pixels and respects reduced motion. Manual review found real gaps: focus can hide behind the tab bar on phones and the page language was set wrongly for untranslated languages (A11Y-01, A11Y-04).

17. How much does it cost to run per user? About R1.19 a month per active user inside a bank app in the base case, mostly support; paid daily WhatsApp messages would cost more than the licence leaves, so we use in-app notifications (model.xlsx Model sheet; COMM-03).

18. What does a bank get out of it? Unproven. Our base assumptions give about R30 of value per active user a year against a R60 licence, driven by retention and balances, which are assumptions. A pilot with a control group would measure unpaid debit orders, balances and retention (model.xlsx Sensitivity; COMM-05).

19. What would make you stop? If users read the date as a deadline and make worse decisions, if real-data accuracy is far below the stated range, or if no bank or funder will host it (investor deal breakers).

20. What does the name mean? "Mutengo" is understood by the founder to mean value in Tshivenda. One reviewer notes it may mean price; we are confirming with first-language speakers before telling the story (L10N-12).

Three-minute demo script

Assumes roadmap items 1 to 8 are done. Timing in brackets.

  1. Hook (0:00 to 0:20). "A balance tells you what you have. Tengo tells you until when." One sentence on who it is for: people paid by the trip, the client or the till, not on the 25th.
  2. Home for Thabo (0:20 to 0:55). Open Home as Thabo, Tight. Point to the date, the likely range and the next money. "He is covered on a normal week, not on a heavy one." Open "Why this date" and show the inputs in plain words.
  3. The hard case, Ayanda (0:55 to 1:30). Switch to Ayanda, Short. Show that the screen says one consistent thing: Short from a date, two days before her next money, the daily budget marked not advised, and "What helps most" offering a spending change, not a late bill.
  4. Plan and the family loan (1:30 to 2:00). Open Plan, enter that her cousin asks for R300. Show the days of cover lost and the state change. "Lending is normal here; Tengo shows the cost before she says yes."
  5. Trust (2:00 to 2:30). Privacy centre: consent per purpose, see and delete my data, "simulated" label visible. One line on offline after first load and data cost. Name the honest limits: synthetic data, English today, bank account only.
  6. Evidence and model (2:30 to 3:00). One slide: backtest MAE 2.1 days, 72 percent in range, on synthetic data with the caveat. Business: bank licence per active user with a pilot fee; next step a six-person test and a bank pilot with a control group.

Handling weak spots if they come up. If items are not fixed in time, do not demo Ayanda's Home (use Thabo only), avoid the language selector, and say plainly that the safe figures are being renamed. Never claim real users, real accuracy or compliance.

Appendix F: Glossary

TermMeaning
BandThe likely range for the run-out date, from a heavy week (early) to a light week (late).
B2B2CA business sells to a business (a bank) that serves consumers.
CACCustomer acquisition cost.
Enough, Tight, ShortTengo cover states: covered until the latest likely next money; covered only on a normal week; runs out before the usual next money.
FAISFinancial Advisory and Intermediary Services Act, 2002.
LTVLifetime value: contribution per user times expected months as a user.
MAEMean absolute error, here in days.
MAUMonthly active user.
NCANational Credit Act, 2005.
Next moneyThe next significant deposit, inferred from past deposits.
POPIAProtection of Personal Information Act, 2013.
Runway dateThe day the balance reaches zero if spending continues at the usual rate and no new income arrives.
Safe to spend todayBalance minus bills before the latest next money, spread over the days until then, rounded down to R5.
Synthetic dataFictional transactions produced by the app's seed generator.
WCAGWeb Content Accessibility Guidelines.
